## Historical pre-Autoplan plan and review (verbatim)

This is preserved history and approval provenance, not current blind-review input.

~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~text
# Design: Preserve the guide concept when switching versions

Generated by /office-hours on 2026-10-27
Branch: main
Repo: forge-trust/AppSurface
Issue: [#183](https://github.com/forge-trust/AppSurface/issues/183)
Status: APPROVED
Mode: Builder
Approved: 2026-10-27, D8/A; engineering review requested with R2-1 carried forward.

## Problem Statement

The reader's requirement is: “I’m reading a guide and switch versions. Keep me on the same concept even if the page moved, with a clear notice. If it doesn’t exist in that version, say so and offer a useful fallback.”

The current archive links to release roots. Detail pages have no concept-preserving selector. A canonical route identifies a page inside one release, but a reorganization can change that route without changing the subject. This design adds the missing switch experience and explicit cross-release identity. It extends the [published-version catalog](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#published-version-catalog), [frozen archive routes](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#frozen-archive-artifact-routes), and [approved alias design](issue-176-version-aliases.md).

## What Makes This Useful

A reader can move from “Initial setup” in one release to “Getting started with configuration” in another and understand why the title and location changed. A fallback identifies itself as a fallback and keeps navigation and search inside the selected version. This is an intended reader benefit; no user study or measured demand is claimed.

## Approved Decisions and Constraints

- Approach B: optional stable `concept_id` metadata for new guides, with an explicit legacy bridge outside immutable archives. This reduces repeated mapping work as releases accumulate.
- Preserve canonical-path continuity where no explicit identity evidence contradicts it. Resolve authored identities before applying that baseline. Never guess from titles, body text, source filenames, translations, or redirect aliases.
- Keep `translation_key` for [locale relationships](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#localization-foundation) and `redirect_aliases` for [routing within a release](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#document-route-identity). Neither becomes a cross-release identity.
- Preserve exact release identity, archive verification, startup catalog snapshots, alias policies, custom route roots, named instances, and PathBase behavior. No runtime Git or network discovery.
- Deliver hosted and static output together. Existing [release publication](../../tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Release/README.md#publish) remains the distribution path. Engineering D6/A reuses `Versioning.MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes` for the new per-artifact switch budget as well as its existing HTML/search-input role, retaining the current default/range and input rejection. D7/A approves bounded hosted guide recovery; D8/A stops static publication before docs-asset/Pages uploads on switch-artifact overflow; D9/A selects a bounded hosted choice-page 503 recovery response, or an empty 503 when that complete page cannot fit.
- The accepted wireframe covers unchanged, moved, and absent counterparts. Insufficient legacy evidence and unavailable releases receive different explanations.

## Cross-Model Perspective

A fresh in-host Codex subagent supported stable IDs plus explicit legacy mappings, highlighted stale mappings, ambiguity, and hosted/static parity, and suggested a concept timeline as a later enhancement. The external Claude reviewer was unavailable; this is an independent native-agent opinion, not external-model coverage. Confidence scores, inferred legacy identities, timelines, migration diffs, and automatic split/merge selection are outside this slice.

[Docusaurus document IDs](https://docusaurus.io/docs/create-doc#document-id) illustrate separating identity from URL shape. [Material for MkDocs](https://squidfunk.github.io/mkdocs-material/setup/setting-up-versioning/#stay-on-the-same-page-when-switching-versions) illustrates corresponding-page switching. [Antora aliases](https://docs.antora.org/antora/latest/page/page-aliases/) distinguish historical redirects from version-selector relationships. These inform the design; none is a new dependency.

## Approaches Considered

- A, pairwise release maps: rejected as the main model because ordinary identity would need repeated declarations as releases grow.
- B, stable IDs plus a legacy bridge: selected for simple new authoring and explicit treatment of existing releases.
- C, a central ledger for all identities: rejected because it separates every new guide's identity from its source and can drift.

## Metadata and Integrity Contract

Add optional `DocMetadata.ConceptId`, authored as `concept_id` in the existing front matter or metadata sidecar. Use existing precedence and merge rules; null/blank means unspecified. Trim surrounding whitespace. Nonblank IDs contain 1–128 lowercase ASCII letters, digits, dots, underscores, or hyphens, start and end with a letter or digit, and compare ordinally. Example: `guides.configuration.setup`. Reject invalid values rather than silently changing their spelling. Renaming or moving a guide retains its ID; a different subject receives a different ID. An ID is independent of a release, URL, title, and language.

Require at most one public canonical page per `(instance, release, locale, concept_id)`. Locale variants may share an ID. Preserve the selected locale using the existing normalized locale identity; do not silently change language. A split or merge needs an authored canonical hub that preserves the old concept, or recovery; no automatic winner.

New exports write `.appsurface-docs-concepts.json`, schema `appsurface-docs-concepts-v1`, before [release integrity hashing](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#exact-version-tree-contract). Each public canonical entry records `canonicalRoutePath`, `locale`, nullable `conceptId`, title, emitted anchor IDs, and optional `sectionLanding` eligibility (default false). Export `sectionLanding: true` only for the actual resolved public-section landing winner, not every raw authored candidate (engineering D4/A). Routes must exactly reference public winners in the frozen route manifest. Physical destinations come from its validated artifact mapping, never from concatenating a route into a filename. The sidecar covers every public canonical page. `identityCoverage` is `complete` only when every entry has an authored ID; otherwise it is `partial`. Missing sidecar means legacy/unknown coverage. A missing ID in a partial index cannot prove absence.

Add optional `ConceptMapPath` / `conceptMapPath` and `ConceptMapSha256` / `conceptMapSha256` to the public version catalog descriptor. Both must be supplied together; the default is no bridge. The path is relative to the existing trusted release root and uses its confinement rules. The digest pins the exact bridge bytes. The bridge schema `appsurface-docs-concept-map-v1` has concept records containing `conceptId` and release memberships. Each membership requires an exact `version`, its `releaseManifestSha256`, normalized `locale`, and either `kind: page` with `canonicalRoutePath`, or `kind: absent`. A page may also declare `fallbackSectionRoute` for recovery and optional `sectionLanding` eligibility (default false); historical eligibility must be explicitly authored in a digest-bound page membership (engineering D4/A). Absence may declare a fallback section but cannot itself be a landing page. The same concept record associates old routes with new identities without editing the old trees.

Every membership is checked against the pinned, verified release and public frozen route inventory. Page and section destinations must exist and be ordinary serveable canonical targets. Membership keys must be unique; a bridge cannot override an embedded different ID, erase an embedded counterpart with `absent`, or assign one legacy page to multiple concepts. Conflicts disable the affected mapping group, never choose by declaration order. Authored publication fails early on these errors. At host startup, a bad optional concept sidecar or bridge disables affected concept resolution with operator diagnostics; healthy archive serving keeps its existing availability rules. A bad release pin or frozen route manifest still follows the existing archive failure behavior.

Do not disclose hidden release IDs, paths, titles, or mapping errors through reader projections. Operator diagnostics explain the artifact and problem; public recovery uses stable generic wording. Catalog and concept snapshots load once per host lifetime. Changing the bridge or its pin requires restart/redeploy.

## Switch Resolution and Reader States

Introduce one pure internal resolver shared by hosting and publication. Engineering D5/A places the combined identity loader/index, resolver, neutral records, and small encoded switch-markup projection in the existing [ReleaseContracts package](../../tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts/README.md) as internal BCL-only core code with no added core dependencies. Keep filesystem staging, HTTP/CSP handling, and DOM rewriting in caller-owned adapters; do not move the entire Docs HTML rewriter or reference the Docs web project from Release tooling. Document the expanded internal ownership and prove clean packed-Docs restore and the Release dependency boundary. The resolver input is a validated source page identity, exact source release pin, normalized locale, and selected target exact release. Its result contains a reason code, optional validated target identity, optional validated section fallback, source return identity, and presentation text keys. A URL projection uses [DocsUrlBuilder](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/Services/DocsUrlBuilder.cs) and frozen physical mappings for every destination.

Resolve in this order:

1. Resolve a selected alias to its eligible exact release using the existing catalog. A hidden or unavailable target cannot be matched. Freeze the selected exact target in the switch result; never borrow a different release.
2. Determine the source's embedded or explicitly bridged concept. Consult that concept's target membership/index. A unique counterpart wins even when the old path now belongs to another concept. Explicit absence wins over path continuity. No matching ID in a complete target identity index also establishes absence.
3. If identity has not settled the result, preserve the same public canonical route when it exists in the target and no authored evidence contradicts continuity. Different known concept IDs, explicit absence, or complete-index absence are contradictions. One missing ID alone is not. This is the retained route-compatibility rule, not inferred semantic identity.
4. Otherwise return unknown when evidence is incomplete. Conflicting identity evidence returns an ambiguous recovery reason. Never turn either into “removed” or pick a similar page.

All resolution and absence checks are locale-specific. A counterpart available only in another locale is not a match. Explicit absence for the selected locale, or no matching ID in that locale within a complete target identity index, produces known absence; partial or legacy evidence produces unknown. The notice names both target version and selected language. Canonical-path compatibility also requires an established target locale matching the source; unverifiable locale identity produces unknown. If the target has no documentation in the selected locale, omit section, search, and home actions that would change language; offer the public version archive and original guide instead. Never switch to the target's default language implicitly.

| Outcome | Reader behavior |
| --- | --- |
| Same canonical counterpart | Open the target page; the selected exact version stays visible. No move warning. |
| Explicit same concept at a different route or title | Open the target page with a notice above the title: “This guide has a different location in 2.0.” Show the original title/version and a return link. A title-only change uses “This guide has a different title in 2.0.” Comparing two endpoints does not claim when the change first happened. |
| Known absent | Show “This guide is not available in 1.0.” Explain that it has no documented counterpart in that release, without claiming that the product feature itself is absent. |
| Unknown or ambiguous | Show “No known counterpart in 1.0.” Explain that available identity metadata does not establish one. |
| Target unavailable | Show a distinct availability message and archive/return actions; do not claim that this guide is missing. Hidden targets receive a generic unavailable result with no hidden metadata. |

Recovery stays in the selected available version and locale. Offer a valid authored `fallbackSectionRoute` first. Otherwise select only a public, serveable target-release/selected-locale page with verified `sectionLanding` eligibility whose canonical path is a strict non-root ancestor of the source canonical path; ignore fragments and use existing route normalization/comparison. Choose the ancestor with the most path segments. Missing or ambiguous eligibility yields no section action, followed by the existing search/home ladder (engineering D4/A). Label this as browsing recovery, not a counterpart. Then offer version-local search when that release has valid search, and its documentation home. Omit unavailable actions rather than linking to live or source-version search. Always offer the original verified guide as a separate return action. Invalid mapping data uses unknown recovery, never a broken target link. Required proof includes authored override, nearest qualified ancestor, ordinary-page/root exclusion, missing legacy flags, ambiguity, and locale isolation.

Preserve the source fragment only when it names an anchor recorded in the target's emitted anchor inventory. A target's canonical fragment takes precedence. Otherwise land at the target page top. Clear unrelated source query parameters. Canonical tags always identify the exact target page and exclude transition context.

## Hosted and Static Presentation

Keep the control beside the documentation identity, show the exact selected version, and use encoded plain text for all labels and notices. The archived guide body remains release-specific. Navigation, search, assets, and canonical metadata use the target tree; only the explicit return action crosses back.

Build a public, deterministic switch plan from eligible catalog entries and validated identity snapshots. Source keys are SHA-256 identifiers of instance, exact release pin, locale, and canonical route; they are public lookup keys, not authorization tokens. The plan exposes only public page projections. Precompute identity dictionaries and embed only each page's required validated projection as inert, encoded JSON. The runtime performs no plan fetch, network lookup, or scan of archived HTML. Engineering D3/A approved this page-local data delivery while retaining `connect-src 'none'`.

Use a packaged external switch runtime, authorized only by its build-owned exact SHA-256 in CSP and matching `integrity` attribute (engineering D3/A). Enable sandbox scripts only when that verified package runtime is present; add no host-wide script source, `unsafe-inline`, `unsafe-eval`, or dynamic code loading. Keep `connect-src 'none'` and the existing sanitizer. Enhanced version links go directly to the resolved target with `docsFrom=<sourceKey>` as optional transition context. The destination derives notice and return data from its embedded validated projection and its own identity; it never trusts a caller-provided URL or notice. Unknown keys, keys with no valid relationship to that destination, and stale pins produce no claimed match. Hosted rendering can emit the validated notice immediately; static output adds it above the title through the same runtime. A bad context must not prevent the destination guide from being read. Full-page loads and RazorWire navigation both initialize the notice accessibly; keyboard selection and focus behavior receive browser checks. Include hosted/static and negative CSP regression proof for the packaged runtime, its integrity attribute, blocked unrelated/changed scripts, and blocked connections. The exact runtime hash must be updated and verified with each runtime change.

Provide a plain-link alternative at `{RouteRootPath}/versions/switch/{sourceKey}.html`, one generated version-choice page per source page, with results for eligible target versions. Each result states any move or recovery condition before its counterpart/section/search/home links. This supplies the complete flow without JavaScript, with an extra confirmation link before opening a moved counterpart. Enhanced controls bypass that extra page. The reserved switch namespace is checked for collisions during assembly. Query transition context is optional; direct guide links still work.

For a hosted standalone choice request with a verified public, still-readable source guide, engineering D9/A selects HTTP 503 on switch-artifact overflow. Return a complete recovery page within the same artifact budget: “Version switching is unavailable for this guide.” Include only independently verified public archive and original-guide return links. If the complete page cannot fit, return an empty 503 response; never emit partial choices or infer concept absence from rejected data. Use existing operator diagnostics and retain the existing source-availability, input-rejection and runtime/CSP rules. This does not change static publication's D8/A failure gate or promise an automatic recovery time.

Hosted output extends the existing verified HTML rewriter with immutable switch context and keeps input integrity checks before transformation. Original archive files and release hashes stay unchanged. Legacy shells receive the control through that presentation boundary when a canonical public page can be established; unsupported HTML remains readable and receives an operator diagnostic instead of guessed injection.

For hosted guide presentation, engineering D7/A rejects an oversized switch artifact as a whole while keeping an otherwise valid guide readable. Show a complete budget-checked notice, “Version switching is unavailable for this guide.” Include only independently verified public archive and original-guide return actions. If that complete notice/actions fragment cannot fit, omit the optional UI and retain existing navigation; log the failure through the operator diagnostic boundary. Unusable metadata cannot establish concept absence or restore a quarantined compatibility match. Preserve the old HTML/search-input rejection and D3/A runtime/CSP rules. Standalone choice-page overflow and static publication disposition are not selected by this hosted recovery decision.

Static release assembly consumes the same resolver and generates the plan, choice pages, packaged runtime, and transformed serving copies. Keep original exact archives separately as verification inputs. Record a distinct serving-output inventory binding each derived output to its source release pins and transformation version. Never label transformed bytes with the original archive hash. Validate all generated destination links beneath the selected mount, including aliases, custom roots, and subpath deployments. This serving-output stage is required for static support, not an assumed hosted endpoint.

Engineering D8/A requires static publication to fail with an operator diagnostic before docs-asset or Pages uploads when a new switch artifact exceeds the approved limit. Preserve original verification inputs and previously published content; do not publish a partial serving inventory or replace affected static surfaces with degraded recovery outputs. Maintain the current rejection of invalid authored metadata and digest failures. Maintainers must reduce the artifact or deliberately adjust the existing option within its unchanged supported range before retrying. Verify the failure gate and document the correction path; this review does not run a publication or upload.

Accepted wireframe: [repository image](https://github.com/forge-trust/AppSurface/blob/1bdac5ca3c6e0f99c9f3dd1b3d5eaaf31c948cb0/docs/designs/issue-183-version-switch-wireframe.png). The full local preview is `/Users/andrew/.codex/visualizations/2026/10/24/01a16cc8-0b72-79a2-8ec6-fae615ab3144/issue-183-wireframe.png`. It is a layout sketch; its example versions and guide text are illustrative.

## Success Criteria and Verification

- A two-release fixture demonstrates unchanged paths, moved paths, renamed titles, a reused old path belonging to a different ID, explicit absence, partial legacy uncertainty, and unavailable targets. Each produces the documented reason and notice.
- Cover duplicate IDs, malformed sidecars, conflicting bridge memberships, stale release/bridge digests, unsafe destinations, missing physical files, hidden targets, locale separation, a counterpart only in another locale, a target without the selected locale, and canonical/fragment handling through supported test seams.
- Run the same resolver fixtures against hosted output and a served static publication. Follow enhanced and plain-link flows; verify keyboard behavior and notice placement on full and partial navigation. Inspect target-local navigation/search and exact canonical URLs.
- Verify original archive inventories and SHA-256 values remain unchanged after serving-output generation. Reject broken authored targets during export/assembly. Exercise valid and invalid custom roots, named instances, alias entry points, and PathBase.
- Document public metadata/catalog additions, the sidecar and bridge schemas, resolver outcomes, publication artifacts, authoring decisions, and pitfalls in package/repository guides. Add regression tests, formatting, and relevant solution coverage under the [repository contribution rules](../../AGENTS.md).

These are implementation acceptance criteria. This office-hours session performs source review and wireframe rendering only; it does not claim passing feature tests.

## Distribution and Next Steps

Ship coordinated Docs, export, and Release-tool changes through the existing package/release pipeline. Older archives stay valid without concept metadata and receive route continuity or honest unknown recovery. No alias-policy redesign, hot reload, cross-version search, fuzzy matching, broad archive-model unification, or migration timeline is included.

1. Define and document the metadata, sidecar, bridge, validation, and resolver contracts with small immutable fixture releases.
2. Wire the hosted selector, notices, and recovery to that resolver and existing URL/rewrite boundaries.
3. Implement static serving-output generation and plain-link choice pages from the same plan, then verify both surfaces together.

No product decision remains open. Engineering review should confirm schema/API compatibility, bounded plan loading and generated-output size, and the presentation insertion seam before implementation. This document does not authorize implementation or a PR.

## The Assignment

Before building, choose two real published releases and one guide that moved or changed title. Record their canonical routes and pinned release manifests, write the smallest explicit bridge that links them, and identify one genuinely absent or unknown counterpart. Walk those cases through the accepted wireframe and check that every fallback names an existing destination in the selected release. This tests the authoring burden and wording against real archives.

## What I noticed about how you think

- “Keep me on the same concept even if the page moved” makes the reader's intent the identity that matters; the current URL is supporting evidence.
- “If it doesn’t exist in that version, say so and offer a useful fallback” requires both an honest explanation and somewhere useful to go. The selected approach and wireframe preserve both requirements.

## Architecture Review

- **ER1 [CRITICAL] (confidence: 9/10), native reviewer, `AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs:899`:** the proposed external runtime is blocked by the current CSP; a network-loaded switch plan would also be blocked. This is a contradiction in the proposed integration, not a claim that existing pages are broken.
- **R2-1 [INFORMATIONAL] (confidence: 9/10), carried from spec review, original design line 77:** automatic ancestor recovery lacked a definition of eligible landings and tie-breaking. Live sections have authored landing winners, while frozen routes omit that fact. Engineering D4/A resolves the plan with verified landing eligibility and a longest strict non-root ancestor rule; implementation proof remains required.
- **ER2 [INFORMATIONAL] (confidence: 9/10 for the source boundary), native reviewer and independent in-host audit:** package placement was open. ReleaseContracts is already shared by both callers and contains internal release-content composition; a full Docs web-project dependency is unnecessary. Engineering D5/A accepts internal BCL-only core/projection code there, with caller-owned I/O, HTTP/CSP and DOM adapters, no added core dependencies, and packed-consumer/dependency-boundary proof.

Proposed flow; shared core ownership is now fixed by D5/A:

```text
author metadata + explicit legacy bridge
               |
      pinned archive verification
               |
      combined identity loader/index
               |
      pure switch resolver
          /              \
hosted verified       static-publication generator
HTML presentation     + separate serving inventory
          \              /
       page-local switch data + plain links
               |
      exact-hash browser runtime + inert embedded data [D3/A]
```

Approved failure handling carries forward: invalid identity groups yield honest recovery; release-integrity failures retain existing archive denial; changed static inputs must not be stamped with the source archive hash. The optional concept-syntax degradation rule applies only after the required archive/digest boundary succeeds. It is not permission to accept tampered or unpinned bytes. This clarification implements the already-approved integrity contract, with regression proof required; no new archive-availability policy is selected here.

Dispositions: ER1 accepted at engineering D3/A; R2-1 accepted at engineering D4/A; ER2 accepted at engineering D5/A. No engineering remedy is approved by the scope answer alone. Accepted amendments are reflected in Metadata and Integrity, Switch Resolution, and Hosted and Static Presentation above; feature implementation remains unauthorized.

### Bounded CSP mechanism evidence

After engineering D3/A, a local browser probe used a meta CSP containing only an exact external-script hash, matching SRI, and `connect-src 'none'`. The exact script ran once; the same URL without integrity, an unrelated script, and tampered bytes were blocked. Encoded inert JSON round-tripped a closing-script payload without execution, and a fetch was blocked. Observed result: `trustedCount=1`, `unrelatedRan=false`, `tamperedRan=false`, `injectedRan=false`, `embeddedDataRoundTrips=true`, `fetchBlocked=true` (engine: gstack browse).

Retained source/result evidence: `/Users/andrew/.gstack/projects/forge-trust-Runnable/issue183-eng-evidence/csp-probe-e7ECMd/`. This checks browser hash/SRI, meta CSP and inert-data mechanics only. It does not verify HTTP/sandbox headers, hosted archive handling, static publication, RazorWire navigation, or actual switch behavior; those remain required implementation proof.


## Architecture Review Conclusion

Three architecture findings were addressed in the plan: ER1 at D3/A (exact-hash runtime and embedded data), R2-1 at D4/A (verified ancestor landings), and ER2 at D5/A (internal ReleaseContracts core). No architecture decision remains pending. These are plan resolutions, not implemented fixes.

```text
metadata adapters / frozen canonical facts / pinned legacy bridge
                         |
          ReleaseContracts combined identity loader/index
                         |
          ReleaseContracts pure resolver + encoded projection
                     /                         \
Docs verified HTML adapter                Release serving generator
(source digest checked first)             (separate staging and inventory)
         |                                         |
HTTP/CSP + DOM insertion                 static DOM + choice pages + assets
                     \                         /
            exact-hash runtime / inert embedded data
            plain-link alternative remains available
```

| New path | Realistic failure | Required handling and proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Metadata into frozen index | Merge reconstruction drops ConceptId, or raw losing landing candidates are exported | Extend parser/merge/export tests; export resolved canonical winners and resolved landing winners only. |
| Combined loader/index | Bridge pin or canonical destination disagrees with the selected archive | Preserve archive verification first; reject/quarantine invalid identity data with honest recovery and operator diagnostics. |
| Pure resolver | Reused old path beats an explicit concept match, or partial data claims absence | Table-driven resolution tests distinguish identity, compatibility, absence, ambiguity and locale outcomes. |
| Hosted presentation | An untrusted script/context runs, or a notice survives navigation to another guide | D3/A CSP/SRI/data tests plus real full/partial/history browser flows. |
| Static generator | Derived copies are stamped with original hashes or partially staged output appears complete | Verify original inputs separately, generate serving inventory from completed derived bytes, validate links, and abort failed assembly. |
| Package delivery | Core code introduces the Docs web graph into Release, or packed Docs cannot restore the internal support code | D5/A clean packed-consumer and Release dependency-boundary proof. |

The existing release workflow runs `docs verify-archive` after staging (`.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:313–329`). Keep that check on the untouched verification-input trees and add serving-output validation before publication; never run the original-archive verifier against transformed copies as though their bytes were original.

Give production consumers a read-only canonical-entry projection rather than accepting any alias as an index page; tests use that real internal API through existing friend assemblies, with no reflection or test-only seam.
- Keep detached immutable BCL snapshots after JSON parsing. Do not retain JsonElement values backed by a disposed JsonDocument or expose mutable arrays/dictionaries through read-only interfaces.
- Remember invalid/conflicting identity groups so route compatibility cannot silently turn them into a match. Preserve existing ordinal route comparison, ordinal concept IDs and the normalized locale graph; never infer locale or concept identity from a title or filesystem path.
- D4/A landing eligibility comes from the resolved public landing winner. A legacy bridge can add a missing historical fact; it cannot override a contradictory published identity/eligibility fact.
- Generate the source-key hash from an unambiguous normalized tuple, preserving the distinction between a known nonlocalized surface and unknown legacy locale evidence. Keep caches instance-scoped and snapshot-scoped; unknown caller keys do not create cache entries.
- Use one encoded switch-markup projection from the accepted core. Caller adapters retain trusted route/physical mapping, PathBase, CSP and DOM responsibilities; no options-heavy renderer framework, new public core API, or wholesale rewrite extraction.
- Package the actual runtime bytes whose SHA-256 and integrity values are emitted, and extend asset embedding/export/validation. Retain the existing generated-search asset workflow and unrelated theme/CSP rules.

Preserve their different I/O, failure and hosting contracts. D5/A already authorizes this narrow shared ownership; no second extraction decision is needed.

## Performance Review

**ER3 [INFORMATIONAL] (confidence: 9/10 for the original plan gap):** the original design required bounded loading/output but selected no concrete size limit. Engineering D6/A resolves budget ownership by reusing `MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes` for new switch artifacts. The current implementation still documents HTML/search input only (`AppSurfaceDocsOptions.cs:1219–1228`), and its response writer encodes transformed output without that input check (`AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs:976–986`). The accepted change is planned, not implemented; actual catalog, anchor and generated-output sizes remain unknown.

Required algorithmic work already follows the approved immutable snapshot and precomputed dictionary design: load validated indices once per host snapshot; resolve selected identity by dictionary rather than HTML scans or a retained all-page/all-version result matrix; walk route depth for fallback; generate static pages incrementally. Static link count necessarily scales with source pages times eligible releases. Per-page embedded data includes only the validated transitions needed by that page; unknown request keys do not populate a cache. Do not invent latency, throughput or memory benchmarks.

The D6/A budget counts UTF-8 bytes per concept-index file, bridge file, complete added switch fragment with embedded data, and standalone choice-page artifact. It uses the current effective `MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes`: default `4 * 1024 * 1024` bytes, supported range `1` through `32 * 1024 * 1024` bytes. Keep the old HTML/search-input rejection contract unchanged. Use bounded reads (including growth during read), detached snapshots, and generated-byte counting before accepting a complete artifact; never accept a truncated prefix. Pass the same effective value to the portable core and both hosted/static callers, document the expanded option scope, and classify exact-limit/over-limit cases in tests. No new switch-specific setting was selected; raising the existing option also raises this new budget. The per-artifact guard is not a total-catalog or total-process memory promise.

No database, query index, LLM or background-worker change is in scope. Verify growth and actual artifact byte counts during implementation using the required real two-release fixtures and a bounded scaled fixture. ER3 is accepted at D6/A. D7/A resolves ER4-H: keep otherwise valid hosted guides readable, reject the whole oversized switch artifact, and render the bounded generic notice with independently verified public archive/return actions when the complete fragment fits; otherwise omit optional UI and retain existing navigation, with operator diagnostics. D8/A resolves ER4-P: stop static publication with diagnostics before docs-asset/Pages uploads, preserve original verification inputs and published content, and never publish a partial serving inventory. D9/A resolves ER4-C: for a verified readable source, return a bounded complete 503 recovery page with verified archive/return links, or an empty 503 if it cannot fit. All four performance findings now have accepted planning dispositions; runtime sizes and implementation proof remain outstanding work, not pending choices.

## Decision ledger

### ER1: Authorize the exact packaged switch runtime

Finding: ER1, CRITICAL, confidence 9/10, `Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/Services/AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs:899–901`, native reviewer.
Plan baseline: office-hours D8/A approved an external runtime with the instruction “Do not relax the existing CSP or sanitizer.” Engineering D2/B preserves that scope. The trust-policy conflict is reopened because actual source contradicts unchanged-policy execution.
Runtime evidence: legacy HTML uses `script-src 'none'; connect-src 'none'` at line 31. Modern HTML permits only the theme-bootstrap hash. The proposed switch runtime has not been implemented or executed.

Motivating current code, verbatim:

```csharp
        var builder = new StringBuilder("sandbox allow-same-origin allow-scripts; default-src 'self'; script-src '");
        builder.Append(AppSurfaceThemePreferenceCsp.ScriptHash);
        builder.Append("'; connect-src 'none'; object-src 'none'; base-uri 'none'; form-action 'none'; frame-src 'none'; worker-src 'none'; img-src 'self' data:; font-src 'self'; style-src 'self'");
```

The original office-hours design, before engineering D3/A, said “Use a packaged external switch runtime” and “Do not relax the existing CSP or sanitizer.” [MDN documents hash authorization for external scripts with matching integrity attributes](https://developer.mozilla.org/en-US/docs/Web/HTTP/Reference/Headers/Content-Security-Policy/script-src). This establishes a supported browser mechanism, not browser verification of this feature.

Comparison grid:

| Choice or contract | Current / approval | A: Exact runtime hash | B: Investigate first |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Switch-runtime authorization | Not allowed by current archive CSP; remedy pending | Approve exact build-owned SHA-256 in CSP plus matching integrity, with conditional sandbox script permission | Keep current CSP; authorization remains pending |
| Network connections | Current `connect-src 'none'`; preserve approved security constraint | Keep none; use inert encoded page-local validated data | Keep none; data-delivery mechanism remains pending |
| General script permission | No host-wide permission for archive scripts | No host-wide source, unsafe-inline, unsafe-eval or dynamic loading | Unchanged; no implementation approved |
| Enhanced switch and plain-link coverage | Approved at office-hours D8/A | Preserve both; verify matching-hash success and negative policy cases | Preserve approved requirement; investigate without cutting it |
| Combined loader, resolver, static generator | Approved engineering D2/B | Fixed | Fixed |
| Archive integrity and locale behavior | Approved office-hours D8/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| R2-1 ancestor fallback | Pending | Pending | Pending |
| Package placement | Pending | Pending | Pending |
| Work and bounds | No feature implementation authorized | Future plan change with hosted/static and negative CSP proof; incremental estimate human 0.5–1 day / agent 1–3 hours | One read-only compatibility probe plus source review, then return; estimate human 1–2 hours / agent 15–30 minutes; no conditional implementation |

Question D3:
D3 — Authorize only the packaged switch runtime?
Project: AppSurface #183, approved design on main.

Hosted archives currently block the proposed enhancement. AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs:899–901 allows only the known theme-bootstrap hash and sets connect-src 'none'; legacy pages use script-src 'none' at line 31. The design calls for an external switch runtime and partitioned plan data at lines 85–87. The external runtime cannot run as written, and fetching a plan would also be blocked.

Recommendation: A because an exact runtime hash preserves the direct switch experience while keeping arbitrary scripts and network connections blocked.

Note: options differ in approach, not coverage. This is plan approval only; the combined loader, resolver, static generator, reader states, archive integrity, and locale rules remain fixed. R2-1 and package placement remain pending.

Pros / cons:
A) Exact runtime hash (recommended)
Allow the build-owned switch runtime by its exact SHA-256 hash in CSP and matching integrity attribute, enabling sandbox scripts only when that verified package runtime is present. Embed the page's validated switch data as inert encoded JSON, keep connect-src 'none', and add no host-wide script permission, unsafe-inline, unsafe-eval, or dynamic code loading. This keeps the enhanced flow and rejects changed or unrelated scripts, but adds a packaged-script trust entry that must be updated and verified with each runtime change. Include hosted/static and negative CSP regression proof; incremental effort estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) Investigate first
Keep runtime authorization pending and the current CSP unchanged. Perform one bounded read-only browser compatibility probe plus a source review of alternatives, then return with evidence; do not implement a conditional remedy or cut the approved enhanced flow. This can expose compatibility limits before selecting the mechanism, but delays the architecture decision; investigation estimate: human team 1–2 hours / agent-assisted 15–30 minutes.

Net: A approves one exact packaged runtime in the plan; B gathers evidence before choosing. Neither resolves R2-1 or authorizes feature implementation.

Embed validated page-local switch data as inert encoded JSON; retain connect-src 'none', no host-wide script source, no unsafe-inline, no unsafe-eval, and no dynamic code loading. Preserve enhanced/plain-link coverage and carry hosted/static and negative CSP regression proof into the implementation plan. Retain the combined-loader arrangement, archive integrity and locale rules. R2-1 and package placement remain pending. This approves the plan amendment only, not implementation.

### R2-1: Deterministic section recovery

Finding: R2-1, INFORMATIONAL, confidence 9/10, native reviewer carrying the independent spec-review concern. Motivating plan text, design line 77: “otherwise a nearest existing canonical ancestor landing page may be offered.” The final spec review left this concern open. Office-hours D8/A and engineering D2/B/D3/A explicitly carried it forward, without choosing a remedy.

Plan baseline: an explicitly authored section is first; an optional nearest ancestor landing may follow; selected-version search/home and the original guide return remain required where valid. No automatic eligibility/tie-breaking rule has been approved.

Runtime evidence: `DocMetadata.SectionLanding` exists at `Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/Models/DocModels.cs:109`. `DocAggregator.ResolveSectionLandingDoc` at lines 2535–2561 chooses the authored landing among visible canonical pages by existing order and canonical-route rules. `AppSurfaceDocsFrozenRouteManifest.cs:563–569` stores routes, aliases and artifact mapping, not landing eligibility. This is an integration gap in the plan, not observed broken runtime behavior.

Motivating existing source, verbatim:

```csharp
        var landingCandidates = sectionDocs
            .Where(doc => doc.Metadata?.SectionLanding == true)
            .OrderBy(doc => doc.Metadata?.Order ?? int.MaxValue)
            .ThenBy(doc => GetSnapshotCanonicalPath(doc), StringComparer.OrdinalIgnoreCase)
            .ToList();
```

Comparison grid:

| Choice or contract | Current / approval | A: Verified ancestor landings | B: Authored section only |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Explicit section priority | Authored `fallbackSectionRoute` first, office-hours D8/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Automatic section eligibility | Optional ancestor action lacks a defined eligibility record | Optional `sectionLanding` from the actual resolved public landing winner; legacy eligibility only from digest-bound authored page membership; default false | No automatic eligibility field or selection |
| Automatic section selection | Unspecified | Target-release/selected-locale public serveable strict non-root canonical ancestor of source route; most segments; fragment ignored; existing comparison; ambiguity yields no section action | Omit optional automatic ancestor action |
| Missing/invalid explicit target | Existing validation and fallback ladder, office-hours D8/A | Preserve diagnostics, then use only valid eligible ancestor or search/home | Preserve diagnostics, then search/home |
| Counterpart matching and notices | Stable IDs/legacy bridge; honest absent/unknown, office-hours D8/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Selected language and return | Preserve locale, omit invalid actions, retain original guide return | Fixed | Fixed |
| Components | Combined loader, resolver, static generator, engineering D2/B | Fixed | Fixed |
| Script trust/data delivery | Exact runtime hash/integrity, embedded data, connect-src none, engineering D3/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Package placement | ER2 pending | Pending | Pending |
| Proof and maintenance | Relevant regression proof required, implementation not authorized | Explicit priority, nearest qualified ancestor, ordinary/root exclusions, missing legacy flag, ambiguity and locale tests; extra eligibility metadata; estimate human 0.5–1 day / agent 1–3 hours | Explicit validation plus missing/invalid/other-locale fallback tests; maintainer-authored section per concept where needed; estimate human 2–4 hours / agent 30–60 minutes |

Question D4:
D4 — How should section recovery be selected?
Project: AppSurface #183, carried concern R2-1.

The approved design permits a nearest canonical ancestor landing page when no authored section fallback exists (design line 77), but archived routes do not record which pages qualify as landings. Live Docs already resolves authored section-landing winners in DocAggregator.cs:2535–2561. Without an explicit archive rule, hosted and static output could choose different or ordinary pages and call them section recovery.

Recommendation: A because publishing the resolved landing fact makes the existing ancestor fallback deterministic without guessing from folders or HTML.

Note: options differ in fallback policy, not implementation quality. Both keep authored fallbackSectionRoute first, preserve absent/unknown notices, selected locale, version-local search/home, and the return link. The combined loader and exact-hash runtime with embedded data/connect-src 'none' remain approved. Package placement remains pending. This approves planning only.

Pros / cons:
A) Verified ancestor landings (recommended)
Record optional sectionLanding in new concept indexes from the resolved public landing winner (default false); legacy eligibility must be explicitly authored in a digest-bound page membership. When no authored fallbackSectionRoute exists, choose the eligible, public, serveable target-release/selected-locale page with the longest strict non-root canonical ancestor path of the source, using existing route normalization/comparison and ignoring fragments; ambiguity or missing eligibility skips the section action and continues to search/home. This preserves automatic recovery and identical hosted/static selection, but adds eligibility metadata. Include explicit-override, nearest-ancestor, ordinary/root-exclusion, missing-legacy, ambiguity, and locale tests; incremental estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) Authored section only
Use only a validated authored fallbackSectionRoute for the target release and selected locale; otherwise continue to version-local search/home and the original-guide return link. This avoids automatic ancestor selection and needs no eligibility field, but maintainers must author a section destination for each concept needing one. Include explicit-target validation and missing/invalid/other-locale tests; incremental estimate: human team 2–4 hours / agent-assisted 30–60 minutes. The optional automatic ancestor action is omitted; all other approved recovery behavior stays fixed.

Net: A derives automatic recovery from verified landing metadata; B makes section recovery entirely authored. Neither changes counterpart matching or script trust.

Record optional sectionLanding in new concept indexes from the resolved public landing winner (default false); legacy eligibility must be explicitly authored in a digest-bound page membership. When no authored fallbackSectionRoute exists, choose the eligible, public, serveable target-release/selected-locale page with the longest strict non-root canonical ancestor path of the source, using existing route normalization/comparison and ignoring fragments; ambiguity or missing eligibility skips the section action and continues to search/home. This avoids automatic ancestor selection and needs no eligibility field, but maintainers must author a section destination for each concept needing one. State: approved
Actual answer: engineering D4/A on 2026-10-28: “A — use verified ancestor landings, keep authored fallbacks first, preserve the selected version and locale, and continue engineering review with the approved runtime/CSP rules unchanged.”

Keep a valid authored fallbackSectionRoute first. Otherwise choose the eligible public serveable target-release/selected-locale strict non-root canonical ancestor with the most path segments, ignoring fragments and using existing route normalization/comparison. Ambiguity or missing eligibility skips the section action and continues to the existing search/home ladder and original-guide return. Carry explicit-override, nearest-ancestor, ordinary/root-exclusion, missing-legacy, ambiguity and locale regression proof forward. Preserve the combined-loader arrangement and all D3/A runtime/CSP rules. Package placement remains pending; implementation is not authorized.
History: Spec finding R1-2 recurred as R2-1. The user retained it at office-hours D8/A and engineering D2/B/D3/A. Previous state: pending; actual answer: unanswered; accepted scope: none. Engineering D4/A now resolves its planning policy without claiming runtime verification. The generated office-hours Reviewer Concerns section remains unchanged as historical review evidence.

### ER2: Shared switching-core package ownership

Finding: ER2, INFORMATIONAL, confidence 9/10 for the existing dependency boundary, native reviewer with an independent in-host ownership audit. This is a placement decision, not an observed runtime defect.
Plan baseline: office-hours D8/A requires one pure internal resolver shared by hosting and publication (design line 58); engineering D2/B approved combined loading but explicitly left package placement pending. D3/A and D4/A are fixed.

Runtime evidence, verbatim project references:

```xml
<!-- Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs.csproj:21 -->
<ProjectReference Include="..\..\tools\ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts\ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts.csproj" />
<!-- tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Release/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Release.csproj:17 -->
<ProjectReference Include="..\ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts\ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts.csproj" />
```

The ReleaseContracts project is a plain net10.0 SDK project without an ASP.NET Core framework reference. Its README line 17 states that it already contains internal `UnreleasedEntryComposer`, `UnreleasedEntry`, and `UnreleasedEntryException` shared by Docs and release preparation. Its existing friend assemblies include Docs, CLI, Release, and their tests. Its public vocabulary remains release-link metadata. No new core or generator exists yet.

Proposed callers, not current helper usages: hosted `AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.WriteResponseAsync` verifies HTML before the rewrite at lines 702–707; static `ReleaseDocsPublication.CreateAsync` currently stages a copy at line 110 and would invoke the approved generator. They need one concept-resolution/projection contract, but retain different I/O, CSP, and physical-output adapters. No whole-rewriter extraction is proposed.

| Choice or contract | Current / approval | A: Extend ReleaseContracts | B: New publishing package |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Shared-core owner | Pending; both callers already reference ReleaseContracts | Internal code in existing ReleaseContracts | Internal code in proposed Docs.Publishing support package |
| New first-party package | Not selected | None | One coordinated package with solution/index/lock/docs/pack integration |
| Core boundary | Pure internal resolver, engineering D2/B | BCL-only loader/index, resolver, neutral records and small encoded markup projection; no added core dependency or ASP.NET Core | Same portable core and constraints |
| Caller-owned adapters | Existing Docs web host and Release staging/export callers | Retain filesystem staging, HTTP/CSP and DOM rewriting at callers; no whole Docs rewriter move | Same |
| Public authoring API | Existing approved metadata/catalog additions only | No new public core authoring API | No new public core authoring API |
| Three behavioral components | Engineering D2/B | Fixed | Fixed |
| Runtime/CSP and data | Engineering D3/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Fallback policy, version/locale | Engineering D4/A and product approval | Fixed | Fixed |
| Archive integrity | Product approval | Fixed | Fixed |
| Verification and maintenance | Shared-rule/caller integration and packed-consumer proof required | Document expanded internal ownership; clean packed-Docs restore and no web dependency in Release; estimate human 0.5–1 day / agent 1–3 hours | Same behavior/dependency proof plus new package artifacts; estimate human 1–2 days / agent 3–5 hours |

Question D5:
D5 — Where should the shared switching core live?
Project: AppSurface #183, architecture finding ER2.

Hosted Docs and the Release tool need the same identity loader and resolver. Both already reference ReleaseContracts, which has no ASP.NET Core framework reference and already contains internal release-document helpers (ReleaseContracts/README.md:15–19). The design leaves package placement open. Keeping the shared core inside the Docs web package would unnecessarily pull its web dependency graph into Release tooling.

Recommendation: A because the existing shared dependency can hold this portable release-document logic without adding another published package.

Note: options differ in ownership, not feature coverage. Both preserve the three-component arrangement, exact-hash runtime/embedded data/connect-src 'none', authored-first verified ancestor recovery, selected version/locale, and archive integrity. Public metadata/catalog additions remain as approved; neither creates a new public authoring API. This is plan approval only.

Pros / cons:
A) Extend ReleaseContracts (recommended)
Put the combined identity loader/index, pure resolver, neutral records, and small encoded switch-markup projection inside the existing ReleaseContracts package as internal code, using BCL facilities without adding core dependencies. Keep filesystem staging, HTTP/CSP handling, and DOM rewriting in their current caller-owned adapters; do not move the entire Docs HTML rewriter or reference the Docs web project from the Release tool. This avoids a new package and duplicated identity rules, but broadens ReleaseContracts beyond its current release-note helpers; update its ownership documentation and verify clean packed-Docs restore plus the Release dependency boundary. Incremental ownership/plumbing estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) New publishing package
Put the same portable internal core in a new net10.0 support package, provisionally ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs.Publishing, referenced by Docs and Release. Keep the same BCL-only core, no ASP.NET Core reference, caller-owned I/O/DOM adapters, and approved public APIs. This gives Docs publishing a clearer package name, but adds a coordinated package, solution/package-index entries, dependency locks, documentation, and pack/clean-restore verification to maintain. Incremental ownership/plumbing estimate: human team 1–2 days / agent-assisted 3–5 hours.

Net: A extends an existing shared package; B adds a dedicated package. The reader experience and all approved safety and fallback rules stay fixed.

### ER3: Byte-budget ownership for new switch artifacts

Finding: ER3, INFORMATIONAL, confidence 9/10 for the plan gap, native reviewer. Proposed additional memory/output scales with identity records, anchors, pages and releases; real sizes are not yet measured.

Plan baseline: product approval requires bounded loading and generated-output size but no concrete limit was selected. The existing option still owns only its documented HTML/search-input behavior. D2/B–D5/A are fixed.

Runtime evidence: `AppSurfaceDocsOptions.cs:1219–1228` documents HTML/search input only. Its property declaration is, verbatim:

```csharp
    public long MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes { get; set; } = DefaultMaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes;
```

The default is `4L * 1024L * 1024L` at line 1170; maximum `32L * 1024L * 1024L` at line 1180; minimum `1` at line 1175. `ReadRewrittenFileAsync` checks the reported and actual read size at handler lines 930–947. `WriteUtf8TextAsync` encodes transformed text at line 978 without that input limit. This is not proof of a running vulnerability or feature regression.

Comparison grid:

| Choice or contract | Current / approval | A: Reuse existing limit | B: Separate switch limit |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |

| Budget unit and artifact scope | Current option covers HTML/search input only | Per concept-index file, bridge file, switch fragment plus embedded data, or standalone choice-page artifact; UTF-8 bytes | Same |
| Rejection/truncation | Current input rejection remains fixed; new overflow presentation/publication policy is separate | Bounded reads/generated-byte check; no partial/truncated acceptance; ER4 remains pending | Same; ER4 pending |
| Existing rewrite input behavior | Existing rejection contract remains approved | Fixed | Fixed |
| Total-process memory guarantee | None | None claimed; per-artifact guard only | Same |
| Matching/fallback and locale | Product approval + D4/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Runtime/data trust | D3/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Internal portable package boundary | D5/A | Fixed | Fixed |
| Regression proof | New budget boundary cases pending | Exact and over-limit classification; overflow presentation assertions await ER4 | Same |
| Operator maintenance and effort | New behavior requires docs | Expanded existing knob; raising it raises both budgets; estimate human 0.5–1 day / agent 1–3 hours | Independent tuning plus new public config/propagation; estimate human 1–1.5 days / agent 2–4 hours |

Question D6:
D6 — Reuse the existing byte limit for switch artifacts?
Project: AppSurface #183, performance finding ER3.

The new concept indexes, legacy bridge and generated switch presentation have no concrete byte limit. The existing MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes option limits only HTML/search input, so it does not constrain the extra switch data (AppSurfaceDocsOptions.cs:1219–1228). A large anchor inventory or release catalog could therefore create much larger reads and page additions than the plan currently acknowledges.

Recommendation: A because one existing operator-controlled limit gives these new artifacts a measurable boundary without adding another configuration knob.

Note: options differ in budget ownership, not feature coverage or test depth. Both count UTF-8 bytes per concept-index file, bridge file, generated switch fragment with embedded data, or standalone choice-page artifact; partial/truncated data is never accepted. This decision selects the guard and configuration owner only. New over-limit presentation/publication behavior remains a separate pending item, ER4. All approved matching, fallback, package, runtime/CSP, locale and existing rewrite-input contracts stay fixed.

Pros / cons:
A) Reuse existing limit (recommended)
Use Versioning.MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes for the new per-artifact switch budget as well as its current HTML/search-input role, retaining the existing 4 MiB default and supported range of 1 byte through 32 MiB. Enforce the same effective limit in hosted loading/projection and static assembly with bounded reads and generated UTF-8 byte counts; leave the current input-rejection contract unchanged. This avoids a new option and keeps producers aligned, but raising the rewrite limit also raises the switch budget. Add exact-boundary/over-limit classification tests and document the expanded option scope; incremental estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) Separate switch limit
Add Versioning.MaxSwitchArtifactBytes for the same new artifacts, defaulting to 4 MiB with a supported range of 1 byte through 32 MiB. Leave MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes scoped to its current HTML/search inputs and use the same bounded reads, generated-byte checks and boundary classification tests. This permits independent tuning, but adds a public option, propagation and documentation to maintain. Incremental estimate: human team 1–1.5 days / agent-assisted 2–4 hours.

Net: A shares the current limit; B makes switch artifacts independently tunable. Neither claims a total-process memory limit or changes the approved feature scope.

### ER4-H: Hosted guide recovery after switch-artifact overflow

Finding: ER4-H, INFORMATIONAL, confidence 9/10 for the original unresolved plan contract, native reviewer. D7/A now selects hosted guide recovery; standalone choice-page overflow and static publication disposition remain pending.

Plan baseline: office-hours D8/A requires a readable destination guide when transition context is bad, safe legacy presentation failure, and honest recovery. Bad optional metadata disables affected concept resolution with operator diagnostics; release-integrity failures retain existing archive availability rules. D6/A selects the artifact budget and forbids partial/truncated acceptance but explicitly keeps overflow behavior pending. ### ER4-P: Static publication after switch-artifact overflow

Finding: ER4-P, INFORMATIONAL, confidence 9/10 for the original unresolved plan contract, native reviewer. D8/A now selects a static publication failure gate; hosted standalone choice-page overflow remains pending.

Plan baseline: office-hours D8/A requires static serving copies, choice pages and a separate derived inventory, with immutable original archives and validated destinations. Authored invalid mapping data fails publication. D6/A rejects whole oversized switch artifacts without truncation; it does not select the publication outcome for that new size classification. D7/A only selects hosted guide recovery. Hosted standalone choice-page overflow remains pending.

Runtime evidence: `tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Release/ReleaseDocsPublication.cs:84–94` rejects a mismatched exact manifest digest before archive/staging operations at lines 96–113. The workflow runs publication at `.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:285–296`, verifies at lines 313–329, then starts docs-asset uploads at line 331 and later Pages upload. The existing gate is, verbatim:

```yaml

          CATALOG_PATH: ${{ steps.docs.outputs.catalog_path }}
          PAGES_STAGING_ROOT: ${{ steps.docs.outputs.pages_staging_root }}

```

No switch generator or switch-size publication gate is implemented; there is no observed production overflow. The second review confirmed that the existing sequencing supports a new gate but does not itself approve abort-versus-recovery for size overflow.

Question D8:
D8 — Stop static publication when switch artifacts overflow?
Project: AppSurface #183 on main, performance finding ER4-P.
Reply with A or B.

Static publication must generate the serving copies and choice pages that make switching work without a hosted endpoint. The current workflow verifies staged archives before uploads (release-publish.yml (`/Users/andrew/Projects/forge-trust/runnable__mercury/.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:313`)), but it has no switch-artifact overflow policy yet. We need to choose whether size overflow stops publication or permits complete bounded recovery outputs.

Recommendation: A because a failed publication gives maintainers a clear correction point before incomplete switching reaches readers.

Note: options differ in publication policy, not test depth; no completeness score. Both retain D6/A's byte limit/default/range and whole-artifact rejection, original archive integrity, existing invalid-metadata rejection, selected version/locale, matching/fallback rules, package ownership and runtime/CSP rules. D7/A hosted guide recovery stays fixed. Hosted standalone choice-page overflow remains pending; this question does not select its response.

Pros / cons:
A) Stop publication (recommended)
Fail static publication with an operator diagnostic before any docs-asset or Pages upload when a new switch artifact exceeds the approved limit. Keep original verification inputs and previously published content intact, and do not publish a partial serving inventory. This gives maintainers a clear correction point and keeps successful output complete, but delays publication until the artifact is reduced or the existing limit is deliberately adjusted within its approved range. Add failure-gate tests and documentation; incremental estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) Publish bounded recovery
Permit publication after size overflow only when every affected switch surface can be replaced by a complete recovery output within the same limit, using generic unavailable wording and independently verified recovery actions. Validate all resulting links and record the recovery outputs in the derived inventory; if any required recovery output cannot fit, stop before upload, and keep original verification inputs unchanged. This keeps valid guides publishable during a switch-data size problem, but adds degraded-output rules, inventory states and tests to maintain. Incremental estimate: human team 1–2 days / agent-assisted 3–6 hours.

Net: A delays publication until complete switch artifacts fit; B permits explicitly unavailable switch surfaces only when every required recovery output fits and validates.

        RewrittenFileReadResult readResult,
        string relativeFilePath,

    {
        httpContext.Response.StatusCode = StatusCodes.Status404NotFound;

```

That input contract is not changed. `DocsUrlBuilder.BuildVersionDocUrl` at lines 333–335 projects an exact-version canonical URL and remains the URL authority with the frozen mapping and PathBase. The proposed hosted choice route and overflow response do not exist; no runtime failure is claimed. [RFC 9110's 503](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-15.6.4) and [303](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-15.4.4) semantics were checked as standards evidence; applying either to this artifact-size condition is the proposed policy, not a requirement stated by the RFC. Question D9:
D9 — How should an oversized hosted choice page recover?
Project: AppSurface #183 on main, performance finding ER4-C.
Reply with A or B.

The plain-link choice page is a separate request, so it cannot use the guide rule of simply omitting optional UI. For a verified, still-readable source guide, its choice-page output can exceed the limit even when the guide itself is fine. The new route needs an explicit response; the current empty 404 applies to existing HTML/search inputs (AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs (`/Users/andrew/Projects/forge-trust/runnable__mercury/Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/Services/AppSurfaceDocsPublishedTreeHandler.cs:952`)).

Recommendation: A because an unavailable explanation with safe recovery links makes the failed switch understandable while keeping the original guide accessible.

Note: options differ in response policy, not test depth; no completeness score. Both keep the whole oversized artifact rejected, D6/A's limit/default/range, existing input rejection, source verification, selected version/locale, identity/fallback, core/adapters and runtime/CSP rules. D7/A's hosted guide recovery and D8/A's stop-before-upload static gate stay fixed; this choice concerns only a hosted standalone request for a verified readable source.

Pros / cons:
A) Bounded unavailable page (recommended)
For overflow at the hosted choice-page route, return [503 Service Unavailable](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-15.6.4) with a complete recovery page within the same byte limit: “Version switching is unavailable for this guide.” Include only independently verified public archive and original-guide return links; if even that complete page cannot fit, return an empty 503 response. This explains the failure without truncating choices or claiming concept absence, but adds a bounded error-page branch to test and document. Incremental estimate: human team 0.5–1 day / agent-assisted 1–3 hours.

B) Return to guide
For overflow at the hosted choice-page route, return [303 See Other](https://www.rfc-editor.org/rfc/rfc9110.html#section-15.4.4) to the verified original guide, with an empty response body and a Location built from the trusted source identity. This avoids generating another page and leaves D7/A's guide behavior intact, but gives no new explanation when only the choice page overflows and can lead to repeated attempts. Test the redirect and absence of caller-controlled destinations, and document this recovery behavior. Incremental estimate: human team 0.25–0.5 day / agent-assisted 0.5–1.5 hours.

Net: A explains the failure at the choice-page URL; B sends the reader back to the original guide without a new error page.

## NOT in scope

- Fuzzy/title/body/source-name inference and automatic split/merge selection were excluded by product approval; identity must remain explicit.
- A concept timeline, migration diffs, confidence scoring, cross-version search and default-locale substitution are outside this slice.
- Alias-policy redesign, broad archive-model unification, hot reload and a new public/support-package framework remain excluded.
- Runtime Git/network discovery, fetched switch plans and broad script permissions are excluded by the snapshot and D3/A trust contracts.
- Degraded static publication after size overflow was rejected by D8/A. Reducing the artifact or deliberately adjusting the existing supported option is the correction path.
- Product implementation, new worktrees, commits, tests built for this feature, acceptance suites, benchmarks, a PR, uploads and deployment are not authorized by this planning review.

## What already exists

Reuse the [published catalog and exact-tree contracts](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#published-version-catalog), [frozen artifact routes](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#frozen-archive-artifact-routes), [locale graph](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/README.md#localization-foundation), [URL builder](../../Web/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Docs/Services/DocsUrlBuilder.cs), verified HTML rewrite boundary, and [release publication](../../tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.Release/README.md#publish). They solve route authority, integrity, mounting and staging; none currently solves cross-release concept identity.

The [ReleaseContracts package](../../tools/ForgeTrust.AppSurface.ReleaseContracts/README.md) already provides an internal shared boundary and friend test access. D5/A adds a narrow BCL-only core there; caller adapters retain filesystem, HTTP/CSP and DOM work. There are two proposed production consumers grounded in existing hosting/publication seams. Existing duplicated concept implementation removed: **0 lines**; added implementation/test counts are unmeasured, so no line-saving claim is credited. New tests and adapters may grow the total change. The reliability benefit is one identity/resolution/projection contract; the separation protects Release from the Docs web dependency graph.

Existing xUnit, Playwright, archive-verifier and packed-consumer tooling are the test foundation. The Test Review and QA artifact identify precise missing assertions instead of treating existing tests as proof of the unimplemented feature.

## Diagrams and Failure Modes

The architecture/data-flow and test coverage diagrams above remain the execution map. Add nearby inline ASCII diagrams during implementation in the combined identity-index/resolver, hosted presentation adapter and static generator where branch/order rules need explanation; do not move HTTP or DOM responsibilities into the portable core.

```text
new artifact <= effective per-artifact UTF-8 budget?
       | yes: accept the complete validated artifact
       | no: reject the whole artifact
       +-- hosted guide [D7/A]
       |      bounded complete notice/actions fit? -> render
       |      otherwise -> omit optional UI; keep valid guide/navigation
       +-- hosted choice request [D9/A]
       |      bounded complete recovery page fits? -> 503 + page
       |      otherwise -> empty 503; valid source guide remains accessible
       +-- static publication [D8/A]
              diagnostic + fail before docs-asset/Pages uploads
              original verification inputs/published content remain intact

Existing oversized HTML/search INPUT -> existing empty 404 [unchanged D6/A]
```

The earlier six-path failure table remains required. Additional performance branches and their planned coverage:

| New path | Realistic failure | Required handling / proof | Reader or operator outcome |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Bounded loader/projection | Character counts replace byte counts; a file grows while read; a parsed prefix is retained | E11 exact/over-limit multibyte/growth tables; detached complete snapshots only | Affected switch artifact rejected; no false absence |
| Hosted guide recovery | Optional switch overflow hides an otherwise valid guide, or recovery itself exceeds the cap | E12 fitting/nonfitting notice/actions, unchanged input 404, trusted actions and CSP | Generic notice when it fits; otherwise existing navigation; operator diagnostic |
| Hosted choice response | Oversized choices become truncated links or an unbounded error page | E14 complete bounded 503 page or empty 503; safe verified links; valid guide still readable | Explicit switching-unavailable response when it fits; no fabricated removal |
| Static publication gate | Oversized generated output is uploaded with a valid-looking partial inventory | E13 failure before uploads, unchanged verification inputs, no degraded substitute | Publication stops with actionable operator diagnostic; existing published content remains |

Critical gaps under the skill's failure-mode definition: **0 remaining planned paths with neither handling nor required tests that would silently fail**. ER1 was a critical architecture contradiction and has an approved plan remedy; actual HTTP/sandbox/CSP and feature behavior are not verified. Implementation proof is still required for every new path.

## Worktree Parallelization Strategy

This is a future execution plan, not creation of worktrees. Keep two implementation lanes only where modules are disjoint, using `isolation: worktree` if execution later calls for isolated checkouts.

| Step | Modules touched | Depends on |
| --- | --- | --- |
| S1 / T1: portable identity/resolution/projection core | ReleaseContracts; its friend test fixtures | — |
| S2 / T2: authoring/export/catalog adapters | Docs Models/Services; RazorWire export; CLI Docs | S1 contract |
| S3 / T3a: package and freeze runtime bytes/hash/SRI plus export asset contract | Docs assets/packaging and export asset integration | S2 |
| S4A / T3b: hosted response/DOM/CSP/navigation integration | Docs Controllers/Services/Views; hosted/browser fixtures | S3 |
| S4B / T4: static serving generator and publication gate | Release publication; CLI publication arguments; release workflow/tests | S3 |
| S5 / T5: integrate parity/browser/consumer proof | Docs/CLI/Release/PackageIndex test modules; RazorWire integration tests | Merge S4A + S4B |
| S6 / T6: final reference/decision/pitfall docs and validation | Package/repository docs; formatter/build/coverage workflows | S5 fixtures and merged production scope |

Lane A owns S4A; Lane B owns S4B. Launch A + B after S3, merge both, then run S5 and S6. S3 is the asset-contract checkpoint; the full hosted test suite is not a prerequisite for starting the independent static lane. Lane B consumes the frozen runtime contract and does not edit Docs assets. Shared core, CLI and asset-contract edits are sequential; coordinate any later discovered cross-lane changes before merging. Final package/build-sensitive proof runs at the final unchanged HEAD; do not run package verification and solution coverage concurrently in the shared checkout. This yields **2 lanes, 2 parallel work steps and 5 sequential work steps**.
~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~~
