## Section 1: Architecture Review

Current scope: BASE kept (Office Hours D6/B/D8/A); ARR kept (D2/B); runtime ER1 kept (D3/A); fallback R2-1 kept (D4/A); core ER2 kept (D5/A); budgets ER3 kept (D6/A); hosted guide ER4-H kept (D7/A); static ER4-P kept (D8/A); standalone ER4-C kept (D9/A). C0 layout accepted, C1 copy control skipped provisionally. Deferred new work: none. Rejected scope: fuzzy inference, silent locale changes, fetched plans, new support package and degraded static publication.

The proposed core boundary is justified by two callers consuming the same identity and decision rules. Existing Docs currently owns archive verification, URL building and HTML response policy; Release already owns staging and upload ordering. Keep those responsibilities in adapters. The new dependency is Docs→ReleaseContracts and Release→ReleaseContracts, with no Release→Docs web edge or new package. Internal BCL-only records and projection prevent a second resolver or general renderer framework.

```text
BEFORE: Docs -> verified archive/catalog/URLs      Release -> verify + stage
AFTER:  authored metadata -> Docs export -> original archive + frozen concepts
                                     |                    |
                                  catalog + pinned optional bridge
                                     |
                   internal ReleaseContracts immutable index/resolver/projection
                         /                                      \
        Docs I/O + HTTP/CSP/DOM adapter                 Release I/O + static DOM adapter
            verified original bytes                       separate derived inventory
                         \                                      /
                      packaged exact-hash runtime + inert page-local data
                                  no-JS choice links
```

```text
IDENTITY FLOW: input -> validate pin/schema/public winners -> detached index -> result
 happy: valid facts -> exact target/locale -> notice/recovery
 nil: no optional sidecar/bridge -> legacy coverage -> compatibility if uncontradicted
 empty: empty valid inventory -> only facts supported by that verified locale; no fabricated page
 error: parse/digest/route/conflict -> quarantine affected optional facts; diagnostics; honest unknown
 archive trust failure: use existing archive unavailable/rejection path, never optional-data recovery
PRESENTATION FLOW: verified source -> resolver -> encoded projection -> output
 happy: complete bounded fragment/choice -> hosted or derived copy
 nil: unsupported shell/context missing -> readable guide + ordinary navigation
 empty: insufficient recovery budget -> no optional guide UI / empty standalone 503
 error: oversized artifact -> whole rejection; hosted recovery / static stop before upload
```

```text
SNAPSHOT STATE: uninitialized -> loading -> validated detached snapshot (lifetime stable)
                             \-> invalid optional groups quarantined + diagnostics
invalid transition: request key -> mutating snapshot/cache (prevent by read-only lookup)
READER STATE: original -> selected exact target -> same | moved/title | absent | unknown | unavailable
                                  \-> budget failure -> bounded recovery
invalid transition: unknown/conflict -> claimed absence (prevent by resolver reason/evidence checks)
PUBLICATION: verified originals -> staging -> all derived bytes validated -> publish-eligible
                                     \-> error/cancel/overflow -> failed, never upload-eligible
```

Tenfold and hundredfold catalog growth primarily increase resident indices and static source-page×release link output, not network requests. Each artifact has a cap; there is no total-process or corpus cap. Invalid optional metadata is a localized failure; catalog/archive trust and package runtime delivery are shared dependencies. A new engineer should see one pure result model and adapter-specific side effects, not an extensible platform. No future migration-analysis infrastructure is necessary.

The serving inventory is consumed by static validation/publication; it changes whether derived bytes are upload-eligible and prevents original hashes being misapplied. The smallest useful form is the required pin/transformation/output binding; retain it with that serving generation. Planning records are user-requested provenance, not a production meta-report service. Rollback uses a known verified prior deployment or prior serving generation; redeploy duration is unmeasured. No new architectural choice or critical gap is identified beyond the retained proof obligations.

## Section 2: Error & Rescue Map

New method names are not yet selected; the registry names concrete planned codepaths and the exception mechanisms adapters must handle. Do not invent a public exception API. Specific I/O and JSON failures receive artifact/operation context; programming defects and process exhaustion are not swallowed by a universal catch. No external-service retry is needed in the pure core. Cancellation aborts assembly and leaves outputs unpublished.

| Codepath | Failure / exception mechanism | Rescued? / action | Reader or operator sees |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Metadata parser/merge | wrong type or invalid ConceptId; FormatException/validation diagnostic | Yes, reject authored invalid value; never substitute fallback ID | Export diagnostic; no misleading new release |
| Bounded artifact read | IOException, UnauthorizedAccessException, growth beyond budget | Optional: quarantine; archive: existing failure; publication: fail | Generic unknown/unavailable as appropriate; operator artifact context |
| JSON/index validation | JsonException, wrong schema/coverage/canonical binding | Yes, reject whole optional artifact/group; do not accept prefix | Unknown recovery; diagnostic with pin/artifact/reason |
| Bridge membership validation | stale pin, duplicate or contradictory facts; validation result | Yes, quarantine affected mapping group; publication rejects | Honest recovery; no hidden metadata |
| Resolver | absent/missing locale/conflict/target unavailable | Explicit typed reason, not unexpected exception | Distinct bounded reader state and verified actions |
| Runtime packaging | missing/changed asset, mismatched SHA/SRI | Do not grant broad CSP; maintain plain links | Enhanced control may be unavailable; guide and no-JS route work |
| Hosted fragment projection | encoded output exceeds cap | D7/A whole rejection, bounded notice or omit UI | Guide remains readable; operator diagnostic |
| Standalone choice projection | complete output exceeds cap | D9/A bounded 503 or empty 503 | Generic unavailable or empty response, never partial choices |
| Static generation | overflow, IOException, link failure, cancellation | Fail before uploads; do not emit complete inventory | Actionable CLI/workflow failure; published generation unchanged |
| DOM insertion/lifecycle | unsupported shell or stale navigation | Readable shell; diagnostic; idempotent current-page initialization | No guessed insertion or stale notice |
| URL projection | unsafe/missing target or wrong mount | Omit invalid action, never caller URL | Only verified archive/return or remaining valid actions |
| Packed delivery | missing dependency/asset; restore/build failure | Stop package acceptance, repair packaging | Failed validation before shipping |

Twelve paths mapped, zero planned unrescued silent critical gaps. This is a plan-level rescue map; feature branches are unimplemented and tests have not run. The existing archive input HTML/search 404 behavior remains distinct from new optional overflow.

## Section 3: Security & Threat Model

Public switch routes and docsFrom keys add lookup surfaces, not authorization tokens. Only eligible public exact release/locale projections are exposed; a guessed key cannot reveal a hidden release or select an arbitrary return URL. Authored metadata is trusted only after syntax, digest, confinement and canonical winner validation. No new secret, database, remote service or dependency is introduced.

| Threat | Likelihood / impact | Mitigation and required proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Markup/query injection | Medium / High | Encoded text/inert JSON; no caller notice/URL; E8 and actual CSP negatives |
| Wrong/stale archive target | Medium / High | Exact pins, public canonical physical binding, archive verifier before transform; E3/E5 |
| Script trust expansion | Medium / High | Exact packaged SHA-256 + matching SRI, sandbox permission only with runtime; no broad source/eval; CP4/E9 |
| Hidden release disclosure | Low / High | Filter before public projection and generic unavailable wording; E3 |
| Resource pressure | Medium / Medium | Bounded UTF-8 artifact reads/output, no request-key cache, incremental static generation; total-size limit not promised |

Checks include nil/blank/invalid type, ASCII ID grammar, Unicode title encoding, long input, duplicate groups and symlink/path attacks. Existing integrity and no-network boundaries handle the relevant injection and access classes; there is no SQL, command, prompt or credential flow. Threats are covered in the retained plan but actual browser/header evidence remains mandatory before ship. No newly approved trust relaxation is needed.

## Section 4: Data Flow & Interaction Edge Cases

```text
source tuple -> pin + locale/public validation -> source key -> page-local relationship -> target
 nil/unknown locale -> unknown (no language guess)
 wrong type/long/malformed -> reject metadata, bounded recovery
 conflict/duplicate -> quarantine, no compatibility resurrection
 stale pin/context -> no claimed match; readable destination
 encoding -> plain-text HTML + inert JSON; no executable interpolation
 I/O/timeout -> caller-owned failure boundary; no network lookup
 OOM -> not generally rescued; artifact cap is not an RSS guarantee

publication input -> original verify -> derive -> all links + bytes -> serving inventory -> upload eligibility
 nil input / wrong schema / changed source / cancellation -> failed stage, no upload eligibility
```

Snapshot initialization is frozen once for the host lifetime. Concurrent requests cannot publish a replacement snapshot; request lookups consume the same detached object, and unknown keys do not add entries. Both requests A→B and B→A see identical facts. Static publication validates a complete stage before upload eligibility; the plan does not claim remote deployment atomicity across independent jobs.

```text
                request A                request B              shared snapshot
order A first   acquire reference        acquire reference      S immutable
                resolve target A        resolve target B       S unchanged
order B first   acquire reference        resolve target B       S immutable
                resolve target A        return B               S unchanged
navigation      start old page A         navigate to page B     current document identity=B
                old init resumes        init B                 stale A must not mutate B
proof           controlled lifecycle fixture exercises both init orders; CP6/E8/E9
```

| Interaction | Edge case | Required handling / proof |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Version choice | rapid repeated activation | Ordinary navigation semantics, idempotent initialization; CP6 |
| Notice initialization | partial navigation wins while prior callback resumes | Bind to current document/identity; stale init cannot insert old notice; CP6/E8 |
| Back/forward | previous docsFrom and locale state | Rebuild from current validated relationship; no cached stale notice; CP6 |
| Catalog | zero/large list, unavailable target, no selected locale | Honest reason, bounded output, archive/return without language change; CP3/E4/E11 |
| Static job | fail, cancel, rerun | Failed stage cannot become upload-eligible; deterministic output; E10/E13 |
| Bad context | unknown key or unrelated query | Guide remains readable, no claimed match or caller-directed return; E7/E8 |

Six interaction families mapped, zero unhandled planned outcomes. There is no runtime async fetch/retry queue or mutable background job. Implementation must exercise real full/partial/history ordering, rather than claiming immutable data alone proves DOM lifecycle safety.

## Section 5: Code Quality Review

The combined index/resolver/projection arrangement reuses catalog, route and locale facts instead of duplicating eligibility in each host. Carry ConceptId through explicit Merge reconstruction; export canonical entries rather than alias-inclusive PublicRoutePaths; derive landing flags from the actual resolved winner. These are necessary implementation details already owned by T1/T2, not another abstraction proposal.

Use small validation, counterpart, compatibility and fallback routines if the resolver exceeds five independent branches; the ordered decision table remains the contract. Keep adapter failure policies explicit because guide overflow, choice overflow and static overflow intentionally differ. No new code exists to score cyclomatic complexity or analyzer cleanliness. Zero new independent code-quality findings; no new task from this section.

## Section 6: Test Review

The required G1–G9 diagram and CP1–CP7/E1–E14 value cards remain in the implementation input with exact protects/fails_when/why_new/seam/no_claim text. Their boundary matters: unit resolver success does not prove actual CSP, packed restoration, author intent or live Pages behavior. Retain all requested caller-level assertions rather than substituting helper tests.

```text
metadata/merge/export ---------- unit + real export integration ---- CP1 E1 E2 E5
pinned indices/resolution ----- friend unit fixture tables -------- CP2 CP3 E2–E7 E11
HTTP + encoded UI + asset ----- handler integration + browser ------ CP4 CP6 E8 E9 E12 E14
static stage/links/inventory -- real publication + served fixture --- CP5 E3 E10 E11 E13
navigation order/no-JS -------- controlled Playwright full/partial -- CP6 E4 E7 E8 E9
package graph/restore -------- clean packed-consumer verification -- CP7
error/rescue ----------------- registry row to matching cards ------ all twelve paths
```

Friday-at-2am confidence comes from a real two-release fixture run through both adapters and served static output, preserving original hashes and demonstrating moved and absent recovery. Hostile QA reuses an old path for another ID, adds markup-bearing titles, forges docsFrom and removes the selected locale. Chaos stays bounded: grow a read beyond the cap, cancel staging before inventory completion, block the runtime and interleave old/new navigation initialization. Those are retained E3/E8–E14 and CP6 mechanisms, not a new production fault-injection project.

Use many pure tables, fewer archive/export/handler tests and a small browser/static suite. Avoid clocks, network services and ordering-sensitive sleeps; controlled pause/release points establish lifecycle order. Finite scaled correctness fixtures record dimensions and actual generated byte counts without performance claims. No prompts/LLM paths are touched and no eval suite applies. Zero new independently uncovered behaviors; nine implementation families remain to build, not nine failures of existing code.

## Section 7: Performance Review

The hottest path is dictionary resolution plus route-depth fallback over frozen facts. Startup JSON/pin validation and static derivation are the slowest planned operations; meaningful p99 values are unknown until implementation. No ORM, DB/Redis pool, HTTP connection or remote background payload exists in this feature.

Resident state scales with validated public pages/anchors/memberships across catalog releases; the per-file cap does not bound the number of files or total process memory. Embedded data stays page-local, unknown keys create no cache, and static output is produced incrementally rather than retaining a page×release matrix. N3's total-output concern remains visible. The plan deliberately preserves the existing default/range; no invented benchmark or new total-size guarantee resolves that concern. Zero additional performance decision; keep finite correctness and artifact-size proof under T1/T4/T5.

## Section 8: Observability & Debuggability Review

The plan already requires operator diagnostics for invalid optional facts, unsupported shells and all three overflow surfaces. Make those diagnostics actionable through the existing logging/CLI channels: operation, stable reason, relevant artifact/pin and configured budget/observed bytes when available. Keep raw docsFrom, arbitrary query text and hidden metadata out of reader messages; operator-only context may name the verified artifact. These fields instantiate the approved diagnostic boundary, not a telemetry backend.

Maintain a runbook mapping malformed/digest/conflict failures to correcting authored facts, bounded overflows to reducing data or deliberate coupled option tuning, packaging/hash failures to rebuilding matching bytes, and stale snapshot changes to restart/redeploy. Existing logs/CLI exit status support the first launch; no new dashboard, metric service or admin UI is needed. Host activity tracing can reuse existing request correlation; static stages use existing job context. An operator should be able to identify which verified generation and artifact failed without logging every lookup. Zero uncovered diagnostic outcome; detailed fields and correction paths stay with T3/T4/T6.

## Section 9: Deployment & Rollout Review

There is no database migration. New metadata remains optional for legacy archives, which retain compatibility or unknown recovery. Build/export schemas, package core/assets, bridge pins and derived serving output must agree before enabling the new presentation; no hot reload is introduced. A separate feature-flag infrastructure is unnecessary for this planned optional artifact/presentation boundary.

```text
ROLL OUT: core + export schemas -> package runtime/hash/SRI -> verify immutable archives
       -> generate complete serving stage -> validate links/bytes/inventory -> publish coordinated output
       -> redeploy hosted catalog/bridge snapshot -> inspect exact-version smoke flows
ROLL BACK: switch failure? -> keep original verified inputs
       -> restore prior verified serving generation / prior hosted deployment + catalog
       -> restart snapshot -> smoke unchanged/moved/no-JS/archive links
```

Mixed generations must never combine a new CSP hash with old runtime bytes or use bridge pins for another archive. First-five-minute smoke checks cover exact selected release/locale, moved notice, no-JS recovery and blocked connections. The first hour checks existing diagnostics for pin, overflow or unsupported-shell failures; this is a future manual runbook, not an automation or claimed deployment. Rollback duration and staging parity are unmeasured. Two risks flagged (generation mismatch, snapshot staleness), both have planned prevention and tests; no upload or deploy ran.

## Section 10: Long-Term Trajectory Review

Reversibility is 4/5 for the optional presentation and bridge, but concept IDs become durable authoring semantics once published. Do not recycle IDs for unrelated guides; preserve them through moves, and document split/merge hub decisions. The main debt is maintaining those facts, coupled option tuning and static output across releases. That is explicit product complexity, not reason to build a general mapping platform.

The one-year engineer gets schema/reference/decision/pitfall docs and the pure resolver table in the existing package ownership. Future migration timelines or cross-version search may use explicit identities, but are separate scope and introduce no current infrastructure. C1's skipped copy affordance is not load-bearing. No accepted expansion depends on rejected research, fuzzy inference or a new ledger package. Native authoring and corpus risks remain known limitations.

## Section 11: Design & UX Review

The approved wireframe places the version control beside documentation identity, with moved/title notices above the guide title and a distinct recovery choice surface. The reader sees selected exact version first, explanation second, target-local recovery third, with original return explicitly separated. Keep the guide body dominant; a missing optional fragment never hides it. No generic dashboard/card system fits this reading task.

| Feature | Loading | Empty | Error | Success | Partial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Version switch | No remote fetch; ordinary navigation | No eligible target: archive/return | Distinct unavailable/overflow | Same or explained move/title | Incomplete evidence: unknown |
| Recovery | Precomputed verified actions | Omit invalid section/search/home | Generic notice; empty 503 only if complete page cannot fit | Selected locale/version actions | Available subset, never fabricated counterpart |

```text
reading guide -> choose exact release -> same concept? -> same: read target
                                      -> moved/title: notice + read target + return
                                      -> absent: truthful unavailable + verified recovery
                                      -> incomplete/conflict: no-known-counterpart + recovery
                                      -> release unavailable: availability message + archive/return
JS unavailable -> plain-link choices -> explained target/recovery -> normal guide link
```

Responsive intention: identity/control stacks without horizontal scrolling, notices wrap, recovery actions remain ordinary full-text links, and narrow screens preserve title/body hierarchy. Keyboard operation, visible focus and accessible notice initialization are required; contrast and touch targets must use the existing Docs DESIGN tokens. The emotional arc is orientation→surprise explained→reading continues, or honest uncertainty→a useful selected-version route back. Deep design review follows next as required, and actual rendered design QA remains post-implementation work.

## Dream state delta

This slice preserves documented concept continuity and truthful recovery across exact releases without editing old archives. The twelve-month ideal would include well-maintained identity authoring and practical corpus evidence; those depend on maintainer practice and implementation observations. It does not deliver semantic understanding, usage measurement, a migration timeline or cross-version search.

## Design Pass 1: Information Architecture

Initial 8/10. The guide's subject and exact version must remain easier to scan than switching chrome. The approved sketch already puts identity/control together, moved explanation above title, and recovery actions under the target version. The missing picker commit behavior is concrete because its native-select sketch could otherwise cause navigation on arrow keys. The three things that earn space are current exact version/language, current guide title, and either a truthful transition explanation or target-local recovery. No persuasion/marketing claims, pricing, FAQ schema or risk-reversal surface is involved.

Dsg1, owner T3/runtime: choose an enhanced disclosure of ordinary destination links. Current: approved location but activation unspecified. Options: A button/disclosure with native anchors (recommended, full deliberate activation, ordinary link semantics, small implementation); B native select plus explicit Open button (same coverage but two-step state and form selection to maintain); C immediate on-change navigation (less keyboard predictability, rejected). Autoplan P5/P1 automatically chooses A, classed Taste because A and B both cover the requirement. Estimated human ~2h / CC+gstack ~30min, included in T3, no new feature. Final gate may revise the control treatment; scope and all exact URLs/states remain fixed.

The one visible baseline Change version link points to the existing planned choice page. When the verified runtime enhances it, present a plainly labeled disclosure button whose accessible name includes current exact version/language, aria-expanded and aria-controls, followed by a list of real counterpart/recovery links. It is not an ARIA application menu. Tab follows ordinary links; Enter/Space opens the button; Escape closes and returns focus. Arrow keys never navigate. The menu includes an ordinary link to the full choice page. No other eligible version: show exact version and “No other versions available”; omit an empty interactive picker, keep verified archive navigation. The choice page still handles direct requests with archive/return recovery.

```text
GUIDE                         STANDALONE CHOICES/RECOVERY
identity + exact version      identity + selected exact version + language
Change version                H1: specific result/choice purpose
breadcrumbs                   one-sentence reason
transition notice (if valid)  target-local primary action(s), in approved ladder order
H1 current guide              separate original-guide return
article / local outline       archive action where relevant
```

Final 9/10: deliberate activation and no-alternative state are specified without changing accepted layout. Remaining limitation is rendered verification, not a hidden product choice.

## Design Pass 2: Interaction State Coverage

Initial 8/10. Existing wording distinguishes absence, uncertainty and availability, but component order should prevent those different states looking identical. Dsg2, owner T3/T4 projection: automatically select explicit shared ordering and reason-specific text under P1/P5 (Mechanical, human ~1h / CC ~20min inside T3/T4). Alternatives were to leave ordering implicit or build separate modal/wizard flows; those add ambiguity or chrome without covering more behavior. This is a structural completion of existing states, not new recovery semantics.

| Surface | Loading | Empty | Error | Success | Partial |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Guide picker | No network spinner; original guide/link stays visible until enhancement | No other versions available + archive | Script absent/blocked: ordinary choice-page link | Deliberately activated exact target link | Runtime cannot insert safely: readable guide/navigation, no guessed control |
| Target guide | Normal navigation, no artificial delay | Not applicable: no target guide is fabricated | Bad context ignored; guide readable | Same: no warning; move/title: notice before H1 | Optional switch overflow: complete generic notice/actions or omitted UI |
| Choice/recovery | Normal page navigation | No eligible alternatives: archive and original guide | Availability message, bounded 503 or empty 503 only per D9/A | Counterpart link names target exact release/language | Honest unknown/ambiguous reason, only verified subset of actions |

Concrete component order for every recovery state: identity/exact release/language → H1 reason → one plain sentence explaining what is known → target-local actions in authored-section, verified-ancestor, valid search, home order → original guide separately → public archive when it is a permitted recovery action. Known absent uses the approved unavailable headline with “no documented counterpart”; unknown/ambiguous uses “No known counterpart” and incomplete/conflicting evidence explanation without exposing mapping errors; unavailable uses an availability headline, generic if hidden, then archive/original only; no selected-locale docs says that version has no documentation in the selected language and offers archive/original only. Never use an absent headline for those last states. The first actually available target-local action is visually primary; missing actions have no disabled placeholders or dead links.

Final 9/10. Existing D7/A and D9/A minimum-budget omissions outrank optional visual detail: never force extra text into a fragment/page that cannot fit. No issues are left unspecified for the retained states.

## Design Pass 3: User Journey and Emotional Arc

Initial/final 9/10. No new issue: the approved notice and original-return link already address disorientation without interrupting reading. Native issue 3 reflects ambiguous wording, so Dsg3 reuses the exact plain-link approval rather than adding/removing a screen. “Extra confirmation link” means source→choice page showing the move→ordinary warned counterpart link→target. That counterpart link opens the guide directly; no modal, second confirmation page, form acknowledgement or repeated consent. Enhanced source→target skips the choice page and shows the validated notice at the destination. This clarifies the existing extra stop, not a new navigation choice or new decision count.

| Step | User does | Likely feeling (hypothesis) | Required support |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 1 | Reads guide | Oriented | Subject, exact version/language, reading column |
| 2 | Opens version choices | Cautious | Explicit label, deliberate link activation |
| 3a | Opens moved counterpart | Brief surprise | Location/title explanation above H1 + original title/version |
| 3b | Encounters absent/unknown | Disappointed or uncertain | Honest distinct reason + strongest verified local action |
| 4 | Continues or returns | In control | Target-local navigation/search, separate verified original return |
| 5 | Uses no-JS link | One extra step | Move/recovery explained before the direct target link |

Five-second orientation comes from identity and a short headline; five-minute use keeps reading dominant; long-term trust depends on accurate authored facts and honest uncertainty. These are design intentions, not observed reader reactions.

## Design Pass 4: Specificity and AI Slop

Initial/final 9/10. Classifier READ for guides and editorial recovery lists, with a small OPERATE version-choice control. The primary visual anchor is the guide title/article; product identity is ordinary AppSurface Docs chrome. This is intended for sustained technical reading in the host's existing dark/light preset and the reader's ambient light, not a new light/dark choice based on category.

Primary hard-rejection check: no generic SaaS grid, decorative image, orphan actionless headline, busy imagery, repeated mood section, carousel or stacked-card app layout is required. Existing grayscale three-panel sketch compares scenarios; it is not a production three-column page. No rejected visual pattern is introduced. Primary litmus: brand YES by Docs identity; anchor YES by title/article; scanning YES by specific reason/guide headings; one job YES; cards NO (unnecessary, none specified); motion NO (not needed for comprehension); decorative shadows removed YES (hierarchy uses spacing/separators). These are primary judgments about the plan and existing sketch, not native or outside agreement.

Respect the repository's explicit Inter typography even though generic guidance dislikes default stacks. No product font migration is authorized. Keep CSS variables, visible visited/unvisited links, 16px minimum for new explanatory/action text, comfortable measure and headings closer to following content. No new entrance animation is needed; all content stays visible and reduced-motion preferences are honored. No new independent issue or task from this pass.

## Design Pass 5: Design System Alignment

Initial 8/10 → final 9/10. Dsg5, owner shared Docs chrome, automatically annotate existing tokens under Autoplan's obvious DESIGN.md-alignment rule (Mechanical, human ~30min / CC ~10min, within T3). Reuse --font-inter, --docs-color-surface-raised/panel, --docs-color-border-default, --docs-color-text-strong/default/muted, --docs-color-link/link-visited and --docs-focus-outline; the exact theme's semantic roles remain the authority. One-off dark hex values from the sketch are not a new theming API. Check 4.5:1 text contrast and visible non-color focus with actual rendered theme values during implementation.

Use a shared semantic selector for the reusable switch/notice because both DOM code and styles consume it; do not put non-search chrome into search.css. Keep local spacing in package-owned markup and wrapper-scoped handling for archived nested content. Use 8/12/16/24px spacing increments as existing utility vocabulary, a quiet separator, and no new card system. The named tokens are reference annotations, not new public customization knobs. Existing design-system ownership and T6 cross-links make the treatment maintainable.

## Design Pass 6: Responsive and Accessibility

Initial 6/10 → final 9/10. Dsg4, owner T3/T4 and browser fixtures, automatically specify reflow and focus under P1/P5 (Mechanical, human ~2h / CC ~30min inside the already planned UI/browser work). Leaving “accessible” aspirational would fail the reader's deliberate-navigation and notice requirements. A modal/mobile-specific second navigation surface was rejected because it fragments the existing Docs layout and adds unnecessary state.

At compact widths below 768px, identity/exact-version text occupies its own row, the change control follows at full available width, and notice/actions wrap in a single reading column; do not truncate guide titles, reason text or action labels. At 768–1279px, use a wrapping identity/control row and keep notices in the article column. At >=1280px retain the existing global sidebar/local-outline ownership and a bounded 65–75ch prose measure. Long release/locale labels wrap; use logical spacing for directionality. Verify 320px width and 200% zoom with no horizontal chrome overflow. New interactive targets are >=44×44px; focus is visible and not obscured. Do not rewrite the existing global navigation responsive system.

After deliberate switching, focus the destination H1/main when there is no retained valid fragment; if there is a verified target fragment, preserve its scroll/anchor semantics and focus that target without an additional scroll jump. Attach the short validated transition explanation as an accessible description of that focus target so it is announced once; do not combine duplicate assertive live announcements with that focus move. Recovery/choice pages focus their reason H1/main, with reason/action context in DOM order. No focus stealing on ordinary initial reading or invalid docsFrom, no trap, and no duplicate handlers after partial/history navigation. Without the runtime, normal page navigation and semantic headings/links remain usable; the choice page already states the move before opening a target. Test these same required full/partial/history paths at narrow width, with keyboard and accessible-name/description assertions. This refines CP6/E8/E9, not a new browser infrastructure project.

## Autoplan DX Step 0: investigation before scoring

Primary product type Documentation, secondary Library/SDK and CLI publication/authoring. Automatically selected under Autoplan P6 from the reader's exact requirement, repository README's .NET integration audience, Docs README and public CLI docs. Mode DX POLISH under the driver; preserve all actual approvals and no new platform/API expansion. Brain digests were unavailable in the completed intake; use explicit instructions, not invented audience evidence. Aside readiness NEEDS_ASIDE is reused from this run; official web sources used for comparison.

### Developer persona card

Who: a C#/.NET integrator reading an existing AppSurface guide for the installed package release. Context: they switch documented versions mid-task and need the same concept, exact release and language. Tolerance: quick orientation is a design target, not a measured abandonment threshold. Expects: a visible version control, a useful selected-version guide, a truthful explanation and a way back. Secondary roles: a docs author preserving stable IDs and a release operator exporting/verifying immutable trees.

### Developer Perspective

I am adding an AppSurface package to an existing .NET application. The README points me to a package chooser, while the Docs package describes preview, export and published versions. I am already reading a guide when I notice its version differs from the package I installed. Today the archive gives me release roots; the plan says a detail page does not yet have a concept-preserving switch. I expect a version control beside the guide's identity. I do not want to learn archive manifests just to continue reading.

If the guide moved, I want its new title and a short explanation before I resume the instructions. If it has no documented counterpart, I want that distinction made honestly, with a section or search inside the version and language I chose. I would be confused if an older path silently opened an unrelated topic or if recovery changed language. Those reactions are predictions, not observed interviews. As an author, I will need a small front-matter example that tells me to retain the same ID through a move. As an operator, I need an actionable error when the bridge pin or generated artifact is invalid, without being told to edit a published archive. The actual new UI, errors and elapsed time cannot be observed until implementation exists.

### Competitive benchmark and exact clock

Clock start: first opening the existing published source guide, without prior knowledge of the new control. Clock end: select a different available exact version, reach its documented counterpart or valid recovery, and correctly understand the notice/version/language. Include reading/choice/navigation; no installation or account is required for this already-served documentation flow. Authoring two archives and measuring build time are different endpoints and are not substituted. Observed human time: unknown. Predicted steps: orient, open choices, activate destination, read explanation (four user actions; no-JS visits the explained choice page). No execution timer was run.

| Tool | Start → useful result | Time/evidence | DX choice | Source |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Docusaurus | Existing guide → choose version/current doc | Human time unknown; official docs only | Version snapshots and stable document identity | https://docusaurus.io/docs/versioning |
| Material for MkDocs | Existing guide → corresponding page in chosen version | Human time unknown; official docs only | Header selector; JavaScript corresponding-page navigation with configuration caveats | https://squidfunk.github.io/mkdocs-material/setup/setting-up-versioning/#stay-on-the-same-page-when-switching-versions |
| Antora | Old resource URL → authored target | Different boundary; no comparable time | Explicit page aliases for authored moves, with cross-version caveats | https://docs.antora.org/antora/latest/page/page-aliases/ |
| AppSurface current/proposed | Release-root recovery today → planned concept target/explained recovery | Current/proposed human time unknown; source/plan evidence, no benchmark | Explicit stable concepts + pinned bridge, honest states, complete no-JS flow |

Immediate 0C target action: automatically choose Competitive, 2–5 minutes, under Autoplan P1/P5/P6. This is a target for the exact reading clock, not a measured rank or release blocker. Champion <2 minutes may be plausible but is unmeasured; current trajectory has no measured duration; neither justifies an invented benchmark. Source guide availability, a usable catalog and representative reader validation are verification dependencies. Record this target before 0D or any scored pass.

### 0D Magical moment and vehicle

After the target decision, choose the existing planned two-release fixture plus its ordinary hosted/static reader flow: a moved guide opens under a new title with a clear notice and a verified return link. Use the existing implementation fixture as the maintainer demonstration and the actual version control as the reader vehicle. Autoplan chooses this lowest-effort in-scope vehicle over a new playground/service or a separate guided tour. No new artifact, hosted demo, account, product telemetry or capability is added. This is already CP2/CP5/CP6 and the accepted wireframe; implementation proof remains pending.

## DX Pass 2: API/CLI/SDK Design

8/10 → 9/10; Hall of Fame Pass 2 was read. ConceptId/concept_id follows existing C#/front-matter conventions; ConceptMapPath/Sha256 follows catalog path/digest conventions and remains an optional pair. The internal resolver stays internal BCL-only, not a public API for every application to install. Exact version/locale and typed reasons are more guessable than inferred title matching. No extra network request or custom client wrapper is needed.

D6/A already requires identical effective byte-budget propagation to hosted and static callers. Source inspection found --max-rewritten-file-size-bytes on docs verify-archive but not yet on Release docs-publication. Carry the directly required adapter work into T4, using that same long option spelling on the publication adapter, default 4194304 and unchanged 1..33554432 validation; no independent switch-only setting. Example configuration is AppSurfaceDocs:Versioning:MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes=8388608 (8 MiB), and the planned publication invocation adds --max-rewritten-file-size-bytes 8388608 to the existing docs-publication arguments. Explicitly label it new until implemented. Document that raising it also raises HTML/search-input acceptance; do not advertise an isolated switch override or change old rejection.

The existing command wrapper renders ReleaseDiagnostic to stderr and returns exit 1 for ReleaseToolException (ReleaseCommandBase.cs:69–90), so native failure-channel omission is a plan-documentation/proof gap, not observed exit-zero behavior. Reuse that path and existing structured report output where available; do not add a JSON mode or new error command solely for this review.

## DX Pass 3: Error Messages and Debugging

6/10 with two High CAP6 gaps → 9/10 after explicit contracts; no CAP4 evidence. Full Hall of Fame agent-ready-v1 reference was read. DX1 automatically chooses stable, documented correction diagnostics inside the existing caller-owned envelopes rather than generic messages or a new diagnostics subsystem (Mechanical, human ~2h / CC ~30min, in T3/T4/T6). DX2 separately chooses sanitized unexpected-failure trace/context in the existing CLI/host logs rather than dumping raw exception messages or swallowing errors (Mechanical, human ~2h / CC ~30min, same owners).

New operator diagnostic reasons have stable identifiers and an explicitly planned canonical Docs README `concept-switch-diagnostics` anchor, cross-linked from CLI/Release documentation. Keep the existing unrelated code families. Minimum new reason mapping:

| Stable operator code | What / safe correction on first or last line | Recovery and side effects |
| --- | --- | --- |
| docs-concept-id-invalid | Name field/source and grammar failure; edit concept_id to the documented grammar, retaining semantic identity | Authored input rejected before publish; fix, re-export complete tree and repin; never patch archived bytes |
| docs-concept-index-invalid | Name concept index/schema/canonical inventory mismatch; re-export from the intended source/tag | Optional host facts quarantined; original archive rules retained; replace valid generation and restart/redeploy |
| docs-concept-map-invalid | Name bridge field, membership, pin or conflicting pair; correct authored membership or supply matching complete path/digest pair | No blind conflict winner or compatibility resurrection; revalidate, repin changed bridge and restart |
| docs-switch-artifact-overflow | Name artifact kind and effective/observed UTF-8 bytes; reduce artifact or have maintainer deliberately adjust the existing coupled option | Whole artifact rejected; D7/A guide recovery, D9/A bounded/empty503, D8/A no uploads unchanged |
| docs-switch-runtime-invalid | Name missing or mismatched packaged runtime/hash/SRI generation; restore/rebuild matching package assets | Never broaden CSP; ordinary links remain; verify coordinated package/serving generation |
| docs-switch-processing-failed | Name failed operation and safe cause class; provide compact sanitized trace/local log reference, host request correlation in operator logs | Stop failed stage before uploads or preserve readable guide under existing adapter boundary; inspect, correct, retry once then ask maintainer |

Codes apply to operator diagnostics, not hidden metadata in reader text. Expected validation needs what/fix/code/redaction/channel; no stack trace. Unexpected CLI processing includes compact exception type + sanitized stack frames or a caller-owned local log path; never raw credential-bearing cause text. Host unexpected failures use existing ILogger/request Activity or TraceIdentifier context to correlate the safe operator diagnostic and protected internal trace. This browser-document flow is not a new JSON API: no RFC9457 endpoint, new response header or internal trace in recovery HTML. Reader messages retain stable public reason semantics and safe actions; documentation errors use their applicable what/fix/code/redaction contract. A correct 200 for an otherwise valid guide is not a false success for a standalone operation; the optional switch failure is diagnosed explicitly.

Publication adapters keep nonzero exit + stderr using the existing envelope, with a stable code/doc anchor, failed operation/field, safe values, explicit correction and whether re-export/repin/restart is needed. Local side effects can include disposable staging; exact archives/published content stay unchanged on switch generation failure. There is no automatic retry loop: correct/reconcile first, retry once, then escalate. New unexpected diagnostics contain only allowlisted version/instance/artifact/budget/cause information; no environment secret values, raw queries, credential URLs, full user-provided blobs or unfiltered nested exception text. Reuse any existing structured-report channel; do not invent one.

These are **proposed corrected examples**, not captured runtime output:
- `docs-concept-id-invalid: concept_id in the authored guide is invalid. Fix: use 1–128 permitted lowercase ASCII characters, starting/ending alphanumeric; re-export the complete tree, then use its new pin. Docs: planned concept-switch-diagnostics.`
- `docs-switch-artifact-overflow: choice-page bytes exceed MaxRewrittenFileSizeBytes; switch publication stopped before uploads. Fix: reduce the artifact, or ask the maintainer to deliberately adjust the coupled setting within 1..33554432, then retry once. Original archives and published content unchanged.`
- `docs-switch-processing-failed: generating derived serving output failed (safe cause class); disposable stage incomplete, no uploads. Trace: sanitized local trace/log reference. Fix: inspect the named dependency/path and correct it before one retry; ask the maintainer if it repeats.`

| Error path | Class | what | fix | recovery | code | context | trace | redaction | channel | Before→after cap |
| --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- | --- |
| Invalid authored concept/map/route collision | validation | pass | fail→pass | N/A pre-work validation | fail→pass | N/A validation | N/A expected | pass specified | inherited CLI pass + explicit assertion | CAP6 fix→none |
| Hosted optional malformed index/map | expected dependency | pass | fail→pass | pass + correction/restart | fail→pass | pass pin/artifact | N/A expected | pass | operator warning, readable guide | CAP6 fix→none |
| Static switch overflow | environment/data | pass | pass refined | pass, stage only/no uploads | fail→pass | pass bytes/effective option | N/A expected | pass | explicit stderr + nonzero | none after explicit code |
| Unexpected hosted/static processing | unexpected | pass | fail→pass | fail→pass, reconcile/correct/retry once | fail→pass | pass safe cause/operation | fail→pass sanitized trace/correlation | pass specified | nonzero static / existing host safe boundary | CAP6 trace/recovery→none |

All after cells are plan commitments; feature output remains unimplemented/unverified. Tests must verify each new code, anchor, correction, side-effect statement, retry bound, channel and redaction through caller seams, including an unexpected dependency/I/O fixture. No reflection or whole-repository diagnostics rewrite is required.

Native findings are not an outside-model consensus. ENG2 accepts deterministic union and full-group conflicts. ENG3 retains the actual D6 budget rather than silently inventing an aggregate cap. UC1 carries the factual CSP platform issue to the owner; no original security requirement is weakened.

### Section 1: Architecture Review

Two issues: ENG2 cross-map ownership is resolved by catalog-wide loading and group quarantine; UC1 static security delivery remains an implementation blocker. Each descriptor still pins an optional map, preserving the approved public API. Distinct maps form one validated snapshot; file order and the descriptor used to reference the map never confer authority. A conflicting page invalidates all implicated concept groups so switching direction cannot yield different winners.

```text
Doc metadata + resolved public canonical/landing winners
                 | export before hashing
       original exact trees + release manifests
                 | verified facts + catalog pinned optional maps
     combined immutable identity loader/index [ReleaseContracts internal]
                 | pure resolver -> encoded bounded projection
             +---+-----------------------------------+
             |                                       |
 Docs-owned HTTP/CSP/DOM/URLs                Release-owned I/O/static DOM
 guide notice or bounded omission            incremental derived serving tree
 choice route bounded/empty503                separate inventory + failure gate
             |                                       |
 hosted browser + no-JS                    staged policy + runtime + static browser
             +---------------- parity ----------------+
```

Core types must not acquire HTTP/filesystem/logging/DOM dependencies. Existing archive verification runs before identity facts, aliases resolve to exact public targets, and no lookup is authorization. Immutable startup state and source-key lookups keep unknown requests from growing caches. Document this graph beside the internal index/resolver and static generator where ownership is otherwise non-obvious.

UC1 evidence: [HTML Standard](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/dev/semantics.html) prohibits sandbox in meta CSP, and [CSP specification](https://www.w3.org/TR/CSP/#directive-sandbox) says it is ignored there. The current workflow uploads a static Pages payload (.github/workflows/release-publish.yml:370) and has no configured security-header delivery layer. A meta policy cannot demonstrate the same sandbox behavior as a hosted response header. Proposed owner choice A is explicit static meta restrictions with exact packaged hash/SRI and connect-src none, hosted sandbox retained; B requires a header-capable static deployment layer. A changes the interpretation of the settled security rule; B adds delivery scope. Neither is applied. Blind spot: deployments may already have an external header layer not represented here. Wrong choice can either weaken assumed confinement or add infrastructure cost. This is a factual feasibility challenge, not fabricated dual-voice agreement.

### Section 2: Code Quality Review

One issue ENG4: the new neutral validation causes must not grow into a core-owned HTTP/CLI logging API or two adapter-specific resolvers. Use the existing ReleaseDiagnostic envelope in Release, existing ILogger/request correlation in Docs, and stable DX codes at caller boundaries. Tests should assert public behavior through friend APIs, not private reflection or production seams added only for tests.

Shared-code rubric has two named proposed callers, a cohesive responsibility and a stable internal seam. There is no pre-existing concept duplication to delete; total code grows. Eight red flags were checked: shallow facade, leaked internals, temporal choreography, pass-through chains, split state ownership, duplicate ways to resolve, public importable internals, and hand-synchronized contracts. Keep one normalized snapshot owner and build-owned runtime metadata; use existing repository URL/localization patterns and per-caller DOM rewriting rather than moving all Docs rewriting into ReleaseContracts.

### Section 3: Test Review

Dedicated actual source/test reads preceded this diagram: DocModels/FrontMatterParser, frozen route validation/snapshot immutability, archive ordinary-file verification, Handler CSP and source404, rewriter mounted URLs, Release deterministic publication/digest failure, CLI real export host, Shell Playwright partial navigation and Search history. Existing net10 xUnit and Playwright fixtures provide seams; they contain no feature implementation. Tests below are future GAPs, not passing evidence.

Coverage of NEW feature paths is 0 verified; 10 code groups and the six flow groups above need implementation proof. Existing tests were inspected, not run and not counted as feature coverage. Three newly explicit gap groups are R1 cross-map, R2 Design refinements and R3 DX diagnostics/config/walkthrough. UC1 remains pending for the static delivery assertion; do not substitute a meta string test for actual browser enforcement or label a header-only proof as Pages proof.

### Section 4: Performance Review

One concern ENG3: aggregate memory and total output remain unbounded by a per-artifact guard. At P source pages and V eligible releases, generated links scale O(P×V); if both grow10× this part can grow100×. No production P/V/bytes/startup-time measurements exist, so exact capacity is unknown. Native recommends aggregate thresholds; primary retains D6/A's explicit per-artifact scope with the approved incremental generation and no retained matrix. A new arbitrary cap or option is not accepted and no throughput/RSS promise is made.

Lookup work is dictionary-based, fallback walks path depth, snapshots are loaded once, unknown request keys do not cache, and each accepted JSON/fragment/choice artifact is bounded by the effective existing UTF8 limit. No DB, query loop, N+1 network call or request-time archive crawl is introduced. Use bounded read/growth checks and generated-byte counting before acceptance. The finite correctness fixture records its dimensions/counts/bytes; that evidence is not a production sizing benchmark or new performance release gate. Total capacity stays a visible limitation.

Generate one bounded destination projection at a time, using immutable fact indexes and temporary iteration rather than retaining an all-page/all-version outcome matrix. Reject the entire artifact on overflow, including overflow caused only by incoming rows; apply D7/A readable-guide notice/omission, D8/A static pre-upload failure, and D9/A standalone503 rules as applicable. Extend CP2/CP4/CP5/E8/E11–E14 with two or more incoming sources, counterpart versus fallback, unrelated keys, stale/mismatched pins and exact/over-limit incoming-data fixtures in hosted/static parity. Document the inbound/outbound record contract, destination binding, no live freshness claim and existing budget in internal reference/decisions/pitfalls.
- ENG7 route comparer correction: logical canonical-route lookup, continuity and strict ancestor path-segment comparison use the existing OrdinalIgnoreCase routing semantics after existing normalization (DocRouteIdentityCatalog and frozen canonical dictionaries). Preserve the target winner's authored canonical spelling and verified physical mapping in emitted URLs. A case-only logical route change is continuity rather than a location-change warning unless another actual title/location fact requires a notice. Concept IDs retain ordinal case-sensitive grammar/comparison; emitted anchor IDs and fragments retain their existing exact rules; locale normalization and filesystem/archive confinement rules do not change. Resolve aliases to the actual canonical winner before deriving source keys so alias/request casing never creates new identities. Extend existing route/frozen/resolver parity tables for case-only routes, mixed-case eligible ancestors, segment boundaries and case-distinct invalid IDs/anchors; do not lowercase filesystem paths or accept a missing physical artifact.

### Section 1: Architecture Review

One issue this invocation: ENG6 needs the destination-owned incoming projection made explicit. ENG2 cross-map ownership remains resolved by catalog-wide loading and group quarantine; UC1/A now resolves static security delivery. Each descriptor still pins an optional map, preserving the approved public API. Distinct maps form one validated snapshot; file order and the descriptor used to reference the map never confer authority. A conflicting page invalidates all implicated concept groups so switching direction cannot yield different winners.

```text
Doc metadata + resolved public canonical/landing winners
                 | export before hashing
       original exact trees + release manifests
                 | verified facts + catalog pinned optional maps
     combined immutable identity loader/index [ReleaseContracts internal]
                 | pure resolver -> encoded bounded projection
             +---+-----------------------------------+
             |                                       |
 Docs-owned HTTP/CSP/DOM/URLs                Release-owned I/O/static DOM
 guide notice or bounded omission            incremental derived serving tree
 choice route bounded/empty503                separate inventory + failure gate
             |                                       |
 hosted browser + no-JS                    staged policy + runtime + static browser
             +---------------- parity ----------------+
```

Core types must not acquire HTTP/filesystem/logging/DOM dependencies. Existing archive verification runs before identity facts, aliases resolve to exact public targets, and no lookup is authorization. Immutable startup state and source-key lookups keep unknown requests from growing caches. Document this graph beside the internal index/resolver and static generator where ownership is otherwise non-obvious.

[INFORMATIONAL, P1] (confidence9/10) plan Hosted and Static Presentation: "The destination derives notice and return data from its embedded validated projection and its own identity". Native correctly points out a SHA256 source key is not reversible. ENG6 specifies destination-local incoming rows generated from real resolver transitions, binding public verified sources to the actual destination pin/locale/canonical identity. Nothing in the query creates authority; multiple sources can land on one target without a retained global outcome matrix.

UC1/A runtime evidence is unchanged: [HTML Standard](https://html.spec.whatwg.org/dev/semantics.html) excludes sandbox from meta CSP; Handler.cs:899 builds the real hosted HTTP sandbox policy; workflow release-publish.yml:370 uploads static files. Actual user rec approves meta delivery with no static sandbox claim and existing hosted enforcement. The static adapter must emit policy early, preserve exact runtime/SRI/connect-none and stop assembly if complete valid output cannot be produced. Header-only proof may not stand in for a meta-only static fixture. No new delivery layer is added.

```text
validated source facts -> resolver(source, target exact release)
          actual destination binding matches this page?
          no -> no incoming row
          yes -> bounded incoming sourceKey/reason/verified return record
     incoming + outgoing + markup/data -> complete artifact byte check
     overflow -> whole discard: hosted guide recovery / static stop
     embedded row + destination binding -> docsFrom exact lookup
     absent/mismatched/stale -> ordinary readable page, no claimed transition
```
Dispositions: ENG6 accepted as exact-contract follow-through, UC1/A accepted by actual owner. No new scope change.

### Section 4: Performance Review

No new performance policy choice; retain the prior concern ENG3: aggregate memory and total output remain unbounded by a per-artifact guard. At P source pages and V eligible releases, generated links scale O(P×V); if both grow10× this part can grow100×. No production P/V/bytes/startup-time measurements exist, so exact capacity is unknown. Prior native recommended aggregate thresholds; primary retains D6/A's explicit per-artifact scope with the approved incremental generation and no retained matrix. A new arbitrary cap or option is not accepted and no throughput/RSS promise is made.

Lookup work is dictionary-based, fallback walks path depth, snapshots are loaded once, unknown request keys do not cache, and each accepted JSON/fragment/choice artifact is bounded by the effective existing UTF8 limit. No DB, query loop, N+1 network call or request-time archive crawl is introduced. Use bounded read/growth checks and generated-byte counting before acceptance. The finite correctness fixture records its dimensions/counts/bytes; that evidence is not a production sizing benchmark or new performance release gate. Total capacity stays a visible limitation.

| Path | Realistic failure | Handling / required proof | Reader/operator result |

| URL/context | injected URL/title or stale docsFrom | E7 E8 encoding, current-destination relationship | guide readable; no claimed match |
| runtime/policy | changed script or network execution | CP4 CP5 E9 real HTTP/static browser proof | no unauthorized script/connection; UC1/A static meta and hosted header tested separately |
| optional hosted fragment | byte overflow | E11 E12 whole discard, bounded notice or omit | guide readable |
| hosted standalone | complete page too large | E11 E14 bounded503 or empty503 | no partial choices/false absence |
| static generation | overflow/cancel after staging starts | CP5 E10 E13 no uploads, disposable stage only | diagnostic, original/published content intact |
| lifecycle | duplicate handlers/stale notice/focus loss | CP6 E8 E9 R2 browser/full/partial/history/noJS | once-only description, correct focus |
| operator boundary | leaked secret or unusable fix | R3 cause mapping/redaction/trace assertions | safe exact corrective action |
| package graph | unshipped internal dependency | CP7 packed restore/dependency proof | package gate fails before ship |
